## Reading (to confirm, not to replace his words)

1. **Baseline = r0.178** (the version in shots 1–2). Revisions after it (r.179 → r.187 and the "Range" commits of 2026-10-10/11 on `main`) are suspected of breaking R-CORE and range functionality; restore what r.178 did, keep only what he asked for since.
2. **Three modes:** TRAIN UP (a hit target pops back up), TRAIN DOWN (a timer under the stop light: how fast all three are down), QUAL (as the Army qualification, IWQ Table VI).
3. **A raster per pose** for QUAL — the five TC 3-22.9 positions in shots 3–7 (standing unsupported, prone unsupported, prone supported, kneeling supported, standing supported).
4. **Every target, every mode:** the user must TARGET → APPROVE → FIRE. Targeted = AMBER; approved = RED. Purpose: rehearsal for a drone swarm where everyone targets and approves before firing.
5. **R-CORE history on the range must match the other modes/surfaces** (the shared R-CORE badge + revision panel used on Vision-2525, SoI, Financial, Drone).

Reading: the table's START should be run by the range officer's commands the way its END is (the CEASE FIRE card above) — the readiness
sequence a real Army qualification range gives before the first exposure, in the officer's words: the line checked clear, the firers told to
load (his words: "load and make ready"), the line asked and declared ready, the lanes watched, fire commenced — each command a line on the
same kind of card, the light and the magazine moving with them (cold → ready → hot), the first magazine loaded by the firer at the command,
not before. "Typical" and "realistic" are his measure: the commands a soldier would recognise from TC 3-22.9's qualification tables and
range SOP, not invented ones. This is the next revision after r.204 (his earlier words first); r.204 is in its chain as this is written.

## Addendum 2026-10-12 (operator, ~20:20 CST, verbatim — the rule behind the three sightings)
> basically system myst track each target and if target is targeted, approved, or fire that way the color of button and silloette of target
> are accurate for others when we have multi-player
Reading, in his terms: **the state belongs to the TARGET, not to the player's last press.** Every target carries its own phase —
UNTARGETED → TARGETED (amber) → APPROVED (red) → FIRED — and everything on the glass reads that target's phase: the silhouette's colour and
box, the lit button, the recital, the toast. One target's phase never drives another target's cue. That is the one rule behind all three of
his sightings (18:40, 18:50, 20:09), and r.204's tenth cut implements it for the player under the bullseye (the glass reads the phase of the
target you are on: amber → APPROVE, red → FIRE, unmarked → TARGET, nothing → nothing lit; the silhouette and its box already follow the
slot's phase since r.196's PHASE_COLOUR_FOLLOWS_THE_SLOT).
**And for others when we have multi-player**: each target's phase, and who set it, is what travels between phones — so a target another
seat has targeted shows amber to me, one they approved shows red to me, one fired shows down, and my buttons answer the target I am on, with
his fire gate unchanged (the marker's seat fires; one approval per shot). Today the deck sends per-mark events over the crew link
(`desig`/`approve`/`fire` rows through `decide()`/`ev()`, `tgtSlot` per seat, `marksPersist()` false in a LIVE room), so the per-target phase
exists but is kept per seat; the room's shared view of every target's phase — one table, every seat reading the same colours — is the piece
still owed. Decided (DD-13f, under his 10:25 sign-off): r.204 ships the single-seat reading as cut; the shared per-target phase table for a
LIVE room is its own revision after his range-officer start (r.205) — listed in the register's Owed and in the release note, so he can call
it forward if he wants it before r.206.

## Addendum 2026-10-12 (operator, ~20:55 CST, verbatim — the scenario behind the three-step gate)
> The concept's pretty simple. When you have a team of 20 people, we're going to all target all the targets. Once all the targets have been
> properly targeted, everyone's going to take turns and we're going to all approve. Only after all approved, we're going to fire. So that's
> one scenario, but you have to be able to identify which ones are at which stage of the three-step process at any given time.
Reading: the range is a TEAM drill in three sweeps, not one shooter's loop. Twenty people, one shared set of targets:
  **Sweep 1 — TARGET.** Everyone targets; the sweep ends when EVERY target is targeted ("properly targeted").
  **Sweep 2 — APPROVE.** Taking turns, everyone approves; the sweep ends when every target is approved.
  **Sweep 3 — FIRE.** Only then does anyone fire.
What that demands of the system, in his words: **"you have to be able to identify which ones are at which stage of the three-step process at
any given time"** — at any moment, for every target, which of the three stages it is in, visible to every seat. So: one shared table of per-
target phase (UNTARGETED → TARGETED → APPROVED → FIRED), the same on every phone, each target's silhouette and box carrying its own phase
(amber/red/down) wherever it is seen, a count of the sweep ("18 of 22 targeted · 0 approved") so the team knows when a sweep is complete, and
the gate that the next sweep cannot start — or at least is not called for — until the one before it is finished. This is the same rule as his
20:20 words (the phase belongs to the target, and travels to the other seats), now with the drill it serves and with the sweep counts.
Standing today: each phone keeps its own marks (`tgtSlot` per seat) and sends per-shot rows over the crew link; the room has no shared table
and no sweep count. r.204 (in its chain as he wrote this) makes one seat's glass read the phase of the target under its bullseye — the
single-seat half of the rule. **The team half — one shared phase table, every seat reading every target's stage, the sweep counts, and the
three sweeps as the drill — is the revision this addendum orders (DD-13f, now with his scenario).** Where it sits in the queue is his call;
as written: the captions off the stage (20:44), then this team table, then the range officer's start (18:25), then the Masters' fall, then
R-CORE — unless he says otherwise.

## Addendum 2026-10-12 (operator, ~21:05 CST, verbatim — the memory, the step, and the colours matching)
> system should always remember what silouettes are targeted.  if targeted, approve is next step
>
> if silloeten is approved fire is next step
>
> buttoms and colors around boxing amber vs red should match
Reading, three sentences, two of them already in r.204 and one new:
1. **The system remembers every targeted silhouette.** A mark is kept until it is fired or its target goes away — look away, come back, it is
   still targeted and still owes APPROVE (r.162's kept marks; r.204 makes the glass read the kept mark under the bullseye rather than the
   last one touched). Already in the cut being pushed.
2. **Targeted → APPROVE is the next step; approved → FIRE is the next step.** The lit button and the recital for the silhouette under the
   bullseye. Already in the cut being pushed (his 18:40/18:50/20:09 words).
3. **NEW — the colours must agree.** The lit button must carry the colour of the box it belongs to: an AMBER box (targeted) lights APPROVE
   in amber; a RED box (approved) lights FIRE in red; an unmarked silhouette lights TARGET in the cue colour. Today every lit button glows
   the same green except FIRE, which is red, so an amber target can show a green-lit APPROVE — the box and the button do not match. Decided
   (**DD-15**): the lit button takes the phase colour of the target it is the step for (amber T13.GIMBAL #F0A020, red T13.LOCK #E24B3B,
   the cue colour for TARGET), gated by a row that reads the computed colour of the lit button against the slot's phase colour.
   It ships with his 20:44 captions removal in the next revision, both being what the glass looks like.

## Addendum 2026-10-13 (operator, verbatim — on the LIVE r.206, and three decisions)
> Awesome; seems like we have fixed The Range.

Reading: r.206 is on his phone and the targeting reads right to him. It is an acknowledgement, not a closing: the range officer's opening
he asked for at 18:25 and again at 04:34 has not shipped, the QUAL lamp is still lit green for none of the time he may fire, and r.206
itself introduced one regression (opening the MAP erases the step sentence). Those are named to him rather than left for him to find.

Asked three questions in the same turn, because each is his and not mine. **His answers, verbatim:**

> **(1) The lane signs clipped at the edge — shrink, drop, or let them slide off the post?**
> "Shrink them at the edge"

Reading (**D-21**): an edge sign is drawn SMALLER so its whole number fits **on its own post**. r.159 — his own rule that a sign stays on
its post's line, held to 1 px by `LANE_NUMBERS_ON_THE_SIGN` — is untouched; the sign never moves sideways. `SIGN_MIN_PX = 9` is his own
legibility floor, so the shrink stops there; a sign that still will not fit at 9 px is **not drawn**, because a clipped number and an
illegible one are both worse than none. That fallback is stated here, not assumed.

> **(2) Which revision first — the leftovers, or the range officer?**
> "The leftovers first (recommended)"

Reading (**D-22**): r.207 carries what is broken now — a dark APPROVE that still acts, the MAP erasing his step sentence, the restore hole
in r.206's own new row, and his sign decision. The range officer and the light follow as r.208.

> **(3) The QUAL light: green is lit 0.00 s of the time you may fire and 17 s of the time you may not. How should green behave?**
> "3 red, 2 yellow, 1 green"

Reading (**D-23**): the lamps count him IN to the targets rising, by seconds remaining — **3 → red, 2 → yellow, 1 → green**, then they
stand. It fills the 3-second engagement gap (`ENG_GAP_S`) exactly and keeps his Addendum 13 of r.162 whole: "1 second per each light",
"as time to next set of targets". Today the same three lamps fire at the END of the window, which is why green never coincides with a
target. **One part is my reading and is flagged as such:** after the green second the lamp **holds green through the exposure** — the
range is hot — and leaves green when the targets drop, which is what both training modes already do and what the deck's own comment has
always claimed ("RED wait · YELLOW ready · GREEN fire"). In the 10-second phase rest the first 7 seconds carry the magazine-change words
and the last 3 are the same 3-2-1. If he meant green as a one-second pulse and dark through the exposure, it is one line.

everything else stands on the picture; then the three programmes on it (DD-32); then the control law across the platforms (DD-29, DD-30,

### 2 — the waiting room (two screenshots: shot52, shot53)

> ensure no mention of light code; should look like crypto so users know we need for comms per vision-2525 standards

> check thisnpartnofnsite works eith 12 AsM.  Also Solo runs should be possible or Team Review and ensur works with 12 AsM

shot52 — the WAITING ROOM · R-CORE top (ROOM / TEAM SECRETS / DIRECT LINK). shot53 — its tail (LIGHT-4 · TEAM CODE
with the five four-dot tokens, START · WAIT DIRECT + AUTH).

**Reading — three things:**
- **DD-41 · THE WAITING ROOM SPEAKS THE LANGUAGE OF COMMS SECURITY, NOT OF A LIGHT CODE.** Nothing a user can see
  may say *LIGHT-4*, *light code*, *colour digits* or *laser*. The screen must read as what it is for: the key
  material and pairing a crew needs before it can talk, to the Vision-2525 standard. The internal module may keep
  its name; **the glass may not.**
- **DD-42 · THIS PART OF THE SITE IS EXERCISED BY THE TWELVE AsM**, as the play screens are — the waiting room, the

  (*"PRACTICE ALONE IS CH0 ONLY"*). Solo must be a first-class path — *or* a team review — and both must hold with
  the twelve AsM. This settles the open question of the Capital's authority: the lawn's programmes are playable
  alone.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-14 later — the R-CORE artwork, and the player-count window)

### 7 — the R-CORE artwork, sent as four files (shot59–shot62)

Sent with no words, directly after being told the badge rasters existed only in a scratchpad and not in the repo:
the R-CORE **icon** (the framed reticle) as `shot59.jpg` / `shot60.png`, 1448×1086, and the R-CORE **wordmark**
as `shot61.jpg` / `shot62.png`, 2172×724.

**Reading — DD-51 · THE R-CORE ARTWORK IS HIS, AND IT IS THE ARTWORK ALREADY IN THE REPO.** Verified pixel by
pixel, not assumed: his PNGs and `frontend/public/r-core/r-core-icon.png` / `r-core-wordmark.png` are the SAME
dimensions and, once his black-flattened versions are compared against the repo's transparent ones composited on
black, the artwork body matches to a **mean channel delta of 0.65 (icon) and 0.30 (wordmark)** with a max of 20 —
encoding noise, not a different picture. The ~94 % of pixels that "differ" are background alone: his carry no
alpha, the repo's are transparent.

So the badge is cut from the **transparent masters** — the same pixels as his sends, plus an alpha channel — which
keeps ONE artwork across the deck and the nine React surfaces that already mount the shared badge. The cut to the
badge's own size (icon 112×84, wordmark 252×84 at 2× the 42 px shared logo height) is **byte-deterministic**: a
re-cut reproduces the parked bytes exactly, so the gate can re-cut and compare rather than trust a stored file.

### 8 — the player-count window, verbatim

> Choose number of players must be possible:
>
> System: Turret, Drone, etc
> Solo vs Team
> 1v1, 2v2, 3v3
>
> Use all learnings from Starcraft II multiplayer windows.
> Difference is in future unlock, one player can operate drone turret while the other flies

**Reading — DD-50 · ONE MATCH-SETUP WINDOW, ON THE STARCRAFT II MODEL, WITH THREE CHOICES AND A SLOT LIST.**
Today the three choices are scattered across three screens — the craft on `sc2`, the challenge chips on `sc3`, the
match size and team on `sc4` — and no screen shows who is actually in the match. SC II's lobby is **one window**:
the map with its preview, the game type, and a **list of slots**, each slot carrying a team, an occupant
(human / computer / open / closed), a race, and a ready light, under one host-controlled START. That is the shape.

Mapped onto this deck, with what already exists named so none of it is rebuilt:

| SC II | here | what exists today |
|---|---|---|
| map + preview | the ladder rung — THE RANGE / THE CAPITAL / … (DD-47) | `MAPS` at `play.html:1172`, declared and never read; `rungIsRange`/`rungWord`/`rungSeat` landed in r.209 |
| game type / size | **Solo vs Team**, then **1v1 · 2v2 · 3v3** | `#wrMatch` select (1v1…9v9) + `lobbySetMatch`; `canRunSolo` is r.211's widening of `canPracticeAlone` |
| race | **System: TURRET · QUAD · VTOL FOIL · (later) crewed DRONE** | `state._selCraft`, the `sc2` SELECT CRAFT buttons, `applyMode` |
| player slots | one row per seat, team-grouped, with a ready light | `state.lobby.members`, `lobbyCounts`, `wrBlu`/`wrRed`, `lobbyReady` |
| computer slots | **the twelve AsM** — `AsM-01 Enki` … `AsM-12 Asar`, six BLU and six RED, seats T01–T12 | the `ASM` table at `play.html:680-693` — **declared and never read**; this is the window that reads it (DD-42) |
| host START | `lobbyLaunch` | exists; `lobbyCanLaunch` (`:3346`) refuses `match>1` — see below |

**THE DIFFERENCE HE NAMES, AND WHAT IT COSTS NOW.** *"one player can operate drone turret while the other flies"* —
a **future unlock**, not this revision. But it breaks an assumption the current model is built on: today **one seat
= one player = one craft**. A two-crewed drone means **one craft, two seats** (PILOT and TARGETEER), which is the
pilot/targeteer pair of Addendum 7 and the 2 v 2 HI of the demo-video script. So the slot model is written **now**
with the craft and the seat as separate fields (`craftId` + `seat`), so the unlock is a configuration and not a
refactor. That is the P2-7 addressing work already named as owed. Nothing two-crewed ships until he calls it.

**The honest blocker, named before it is promised:** `lobbyCanLaunch` (`play.html:3346`) refuses any match above
1 v 1 with *MULTI-PEER TRANSPORT NOT QUALIFIED*, and `lib/2525-core/lobby.ts` `QUALIFIED_MATCH = 1` is asserted by
`tests/lobby-2525.test.mjs`. The transport is one direct WebRTC peer. So 2 v 2 and 3 v 3 can be **chosen, shown and
seated — with the twelve AsM filling the other slots as computer players** — and a run of real humans beyond 1 v 1
waits on a transport revision. The window must say which is which rather than offer a start that will refuse.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-14 — R-CORE describes history, and gains a Code mode)

### 9 — verbatim, with two images (shot63 the `</> Code` chip, shot64 the Vision-2525 R-CORE panel on his phone)

> R-CORE SHOULD DEACRIBE history like vision 2525; but also have a code mode that saves version of code for
> traceability, so future versions of AI and Hi can see exactly what happened; use inspirstion for CUBE SIM
> hidden behind easter egg while in `</>` Code mode

**shot64 is the target picture, from his own phone** — the Vision-2525 panel open at
*WHAT CHANGED — V.01 R0.001 → V.19 R1.016 (CURRENT)*: the Before and After scrubbers with their impact ticks,
the `vs previous` / `v1 vs latest` presets, the chips `added 181` · `revised 6` · `carried unchanged 0` ·
`287 releases crossed`, the summary line *187 changes across 29 sections · highest impact: … · removed 0 —
append-only*, and KEY IMPROVEMENTS numbered 01–07 with their L5 chips. That is not a new design to invent; it is
`components/2525-core/rcore-revision-panel.tsx` as it already renders on nine surfaces, and it settles what the
deck's own panel must be.

**Reading — three decisions.**

- **DD-52 · THE RANGE'S R-CORE IS THE VISION-2525 PANEL, NOT A COUSIN OF IT.** His screenshot is the
  specification. The deck's port carries the same parts and the same words — the two scrubbers, the two presets,
  the four chips, the summary sentence, KEY IMPROVEMENTS with L1–L5, the Unified / Side-by-side diff, the change
  navigator, the Details table, the newest-first timeline with its D# and commit citations, and the download.

- **DD-53 · A `</>` CODE MODE THAT SHOWS THE CODE OF EVERY REVISION.** *"so future versions of AI and HI can see
  exactly what happened."* **The code is already saved** — measured, not assumed: the operator deck carries **81
  revision files** (`drone-2525_r.003.html` … `drone-2525_r.208.html`), **49 patchers**
  (`patches/rNNN_to_rNNN+1.py`, each one a refuse-on-any-missed-anchor description of exactly what changed) and
  **53 hash files**, 30 MB in all, with a chain-hashed register row per revision. **What is missing is that
  R-CORE never shows any of it.** Code mode is therefore a second reading of the SAME compare the prose panel
  already makes: pick Before and After, and instead of the word diff of the release text you get the revision's
  own code — its sha256, its byte size, its chain hash, and the patcher that produced it, which is the most
  honest possible account of a change because the build refuses if a single anchor has moved. One toggle,
  `</> Code`, in the panel header; one compare; two readings.

- **DD-54 · CUBE SIM BEHIND AN EASTER EGG, INSIDE CODE MODE.** The house already has this pattern and it is
  reused, not reinvented: `frontend/lib/easter-egg-context.tsx` (253 lines) unlocks simulation with the theme
  sequence `exel-cyan → sunset → violet` inside a 5 s window, and gates **Cube 10** behind the SoI Trinity
  sequence — `ADMIN_SEQUENCE = ["hi","ai","si"]` (웃 → ◬ → ♡) and `CHALLENGER_SEQUENCE = ["si","ai","hi"]` —
  with `registerCube10Click` / `verifyCube10Code` / `cube10Access`. So the deck's egg is the **Trinity sequence
  tapped while Code mode is open**, which is the same gesture the rest of the 2525 surfaces already teach.

**Order, and what it costs.** The prose panel is the foundation and ships first (r.210, his placement: the icon at
the bottom below TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE). Code mode and the Cube-SIM egg are a second pass over the same panel
(r.211). Both are additive to the deck's chrome and touch no frozen span and no painter.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-14 — Supabase, and projects stay separate)

### 10 — verbatim

> ensure supabase is linked
> eventually Drone-2525 will link yo Security-2525, until then, we treat each projects as separate

**Reading — two things.**

- **DD-56 · EACH PROJECT IS TREATED AS SEPARATE UNTIL HE SAYS OTHERWISE, AND DRONE-2525 → SECURITY-2525 IS THE
  FIRST LINK HE NAMES.** This governs the per-project R-CORE programme (DD-55) directly: one ledger, one panel,
  one code record **per project**, with **no cross-project coupling built now** — no shared history, no merged
  timeline, no entry in one project's ledger that depends on another's. The registry row is the seam: when the
  link comes it is a declared relation between two rows, not a retrofit through code that assumed one record.
  Recorded as future intent, not as work.

- **DD-57 · SUPABASE MUST BE LINKED — AND MUST BE PROVABLE.** Measured today, not assumed:

  | where | state | evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | this container | **not set** | `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` / `_ANON_KEY` absent from the environment; only `.env.example` exists, no `.env` |
  | the client code | **degrades silently** | `frontend/lib/supabase.ts:6-9` — `supabaseUrl && supabaseAnonKey ? createClient(...) : null`. No config means `supabase === null` and every caller must handle it. |
  | GitHub Actions | **NOT SET — confirmed** | the `ship` job of Deploy #1875 on `a206c5dd` is **skipped**, which by `deploy.yml:107-115`'s own branch means exactly *"the Supabase build vars are not set as Actions secrets; Cloudflare's git build is the only deployer of this sha"* |
  | the backend "live-database proofs" | **prove nothing about Supabase** | `backend/tests/live_db/` connects to `localhost:5432` — the job's own service container, not Supabase |
  | **Cloudflare Workers Builds — the only actual deployer** | **UNVERIFIED** | the sandbox proxy 403s every host (`curl` → `CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403`), so the live bundle cannot be read from here |

  **Why it matters, in the repo's own words.** `deploy.yml:18`: *"`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` ← required; **Trinity Redundancy depends on them**"*. Per CLAUDE.md's locked
  Trinity table, if `supabase` is null on the live site then **send paths A (Broadcast) and B (DB INSERT) are
  dead, leaving only C (CF KV)**, and **receive channels A (Broadcast), B (postgres_changes) and D (REST poll)
  are dead, leaving only C (KV poll)** — a 3×4 redundancy reduced to 1×1, with no visible symptom until a
  session needs it.

  **The answer is not to assert it; it is to make it checkable on every run**, which is this project's own law
  (*UNREACHABLE IS NOT A PASS · LIVE means the site serves it, proven by fetching it*). Verify Live already
  fetches the live site from a runner that can reach it, so the proof belongs there.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-14 — the redundancy thread, persisted late; see the note at the end)

### 11 — verbatim, in the order he sent them

> now inspire redundancy plan on these two; if that means we need other non-Supabase source let me know

> summarize both Vision-2525 13 section and Vision-2525 19 section in 333 word and 3 paragraphs each
>
> then recommend how we make system to level of 2525 redundancy

> 111 per paragrpah is not necessary; 333 words per summary is
> by 19 i meant Vision-2525 online. which has 19 sections. when mentioning 13, i mean pdf vision 2525 paper

His two answers to the questions that followed (AskUserQuestion, 2026-10-14):

> **"Trinity everywhere — 3×4"** — asked what "2525 redundancy" should mean as a standard.
>
> **"Chat only"** — asked where the two summaries should live. (Superseded by his later instruction below, which
> asks for them as a file as well.)

> remind me about KV LATER tomorrow

> i must turn phone on airplane mode as phone will die ; keep working; until all /project-2525 have R-CORE.
> approve autonomously and let me know when risk is lowered significantly for redundancy

> save the 2 333 writeup summaries as well as .MD for future reference

**Reading.**

- **DD-58 · THE LOCKED TRINITY SPEC MUST DESCRIBE THE SYSTEM THAT EXISTS.** His standard is "Trinity everywhere
  — 3×4": every critical path gets at least three independent ways in and several ways to read back, each able
  to carry the load alone. The honest first step was not to build toward it but to record that **the one place
  that already declares it is not true**: `CLAUDE.md` locks Trinity Redundancy as 3 send paths × 4 receive
  channels, but the receive side is four channels on paper and **three in code, all three terminating at
  Supabase**, and Path C has no durable store because the KV binding is commented out. Annotated in `CLAUDE.md`
  with the measured reality, nothing reverted — commit `ac0049bfe`. **This DD number was used in that commit
  before it was defined here; defining it is part of closing that gap.**
- **The non-Supabase sources he asked about: three, and two were free.** Cloudflare KV (written, routed, and
  disabled behind one namespace id only he can create); **the device itself** (an outbox — shipped, `415874f91`);
  and a direct peer over WebRTC (already proven inside the drone deck, not yet in the polling app). A second
  Supabase project does **not** count and must not be described as independence.
- **The 333-word summaries.** Both written to his correction (333 words per summary, three paragraphs, lengths
  free; 13 = the PDF paper, 19 = the online paper) and given in chat, then saved to
  `docs/whitepaper/2026-10-14_VISION-2525_summaries_333.md` (commit `4527c0efb`) with the provenance sha256 of
  both source documents and a command in the file that re-counts the words.
- **His standing instruction while away** — keep working until every `/project-2525` has R-CORE, approve my own
  decisions, and report when redundancy risk is significantly lower. Done: 0 projects owed (`ff9efbd6a`), and
  independent providers for live response delivery went from **1 to 2**.

**WHY THIS ADDENDUM IS LATE, recorded rather than quietly fixed.** These eight messages were acted on — four
commits and a whole redundancy programme came out of them — while his actual words stayed outside this file,
which is the gated record (`frontend/tests/asks-persist.test.mjs`). His law is PERSIST FIRST: his words are
written down **before** any analysis, planning or request for approval. The work was right; the record was not
kept, and that is the same shape as the failure the law was written for. Appended 2026-10-15, out of date order
and said so, rather than back-dated.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-15 — the Code chip, as artwork)

### 13 — his own answers, then his own rasters

Asked how the `</>` Code button should be built, he answered four questions and then sent the artwork.

**His words on method, verbatim:**

> that way we avoid rework and prevents unnecessary token use that inflates demand of anthropic compute needed

> yes; how we visualize elements (as 1-27 pieces of cube) and then deep dive on a section of code for that
> revision

> Thanks for modular R-Core vision 2525 use of existing code.

**His four answers (AskUserQuestion, 2026-10-15):**

| asked | his answer |
|---|---|
| what one of the 27 cube pieces IS, for a project | **a fixed map of the project's own areas** — declared once, the same shape every revision, with this revision's pieces lit |
| what the deep dive shows | **the change that revision made** to that piece |
| what a piece is for the Drone deck, which is ONE 1.15 MB file | **"follow same SIM and visualization methods as all other projects"** |
| where it lives | **a third mode in the R-CORE panel**, beside Unified and Side-by-side |

**Then two images, sent with no words** (persisted as `…_shot65.png`, 1558 × 1009, 946,278 B, and
`…_shot66.png`, 2171 × 724, 1,391,242 B): a neon-cyan `</>` glyph in a rounded pill, and a wider pill reading
`</> Code`.

**Reading — DD-65 … DD-68.**

- **DD-65 · THE CODE CHIP IS HIS RASTER, IN THE BADGE'S OWN TWO-STAGE LANGUAGE.** The pair is exactly how
  `components/2525-core/rcore-badge.tsx` already behaves — `/r-core/r-core-icon.png` at rest, the wordmark pill
  on the first click. The `</>` icon is the rest state and `</> Code` the active one. **This supersedes the
  earlier decision to draw the glyph as inline SVG**: his raster is the artwork, and a drawn glyph is not it.
  Served from `/r-core/code-icon.png` and `/r-core/code-wordmark.png` — paths that deliberately do NOT match
  `tests/rcore-revisions.test.mjs:123`, which counts `/r-core/r-core-(icon|wordmark).png` and holds the panel to
  exactly one. Their height comes from a named constant, never a literal, because line 126 bans
  `height: <number>, width: "auto"` anywhere in the panel. Both are resampled before shipping — 946 KB and
  1.39 MB are chip artwork at poster size.
- **DD-66 · A PIECE IS A DECLARED AREA OF THE PROJECT, NOT A FILE OF THE DIFF.** The cube keeps one shape per
  project so its anatomy can be learned; what changes revision to revision is which pieces are lit. The areas
  live in `docs/traceability/projects.registry.json`, one row each.
- **DD-67 · THE DECK IS NOT A SPECIAL CASE.** Its 84 carried revisions and 52 refuse-on-mismatch patchers are the
  richest record in the repository, and the temptation was to give it a bespoke shape. His answer forbids it: the
  deck declares areas like every other project, and a patcher's asserted edits become the CONTENT of an area's
  diff rather than a different kind of piece.
- **DD-68 · THE DEEP DIVE IS A DIFF, NOT A FILE DUMP.** It reads the same Before/After the panel already holds, so
  Code is a third reading of one compare — not a second panel and not a second screen.

### 14 — the cube's own rule, verbatim

> so R-CORE shows revision differences and `</>` shows code for those revisions
>
> 27 is max; of code is teo sections; split visual of 3x3x3 in 2 pieces
>
> use adjacent mini cubes geoupoed like lego; method exists already in SIM behind easter egg
>
> make sure drone pieces allow gor future modularization, 1v1, 3v3
>
> different maps like capital lawn, City of Austin, State of Texas

**Reading — DD-69 … DD-71.**

- **DD-69 · 27 IS A MAXIMUM, NOT A COUNT, AND THE SPLIT IS BY LEGO.** A project with two sections of code splits
  the 3×3×3 into **two pieces**, not two labels over twenty-seven cells. The pieces are groups of **adjacent**
  mini-cubes — they touch, side by side or stacked, so a piece could physically stack into a shape. He also says
  where it lives: *"method exists already in SIM behind easter egg"*, and he is right twice over.
  `backend/app/cubes/cube10_simulation/sections.py` has `partition()` (the face-connected region-grow) and its
  own warning at `segment_cells` — *"index-contiguous is NOT face-connected in 3D"* — and
  `frontend/lib/sim-sections.ts` is its frontend mirror, whose header declares it **"the ONE frontend source …
  no forked copy, no drift"**. The first cut of the code record split by contiguous index; the second wrote a
  PORT of the Python, which was a forked copy of that one source. Both were wrong, and his sentence named the
  right answer before either was written. The seed type is widened to accept a project id and nothing else
  changes.
- **DD-70 · THE DRONE PIECES ARE CUT FOR WHAT IS COMING, NOT ONLY FOR WHAT EXISTS.** *"make sure drone pieces
  allow for future modularization, 1v1, 3v3."* So the map ladder, each map rung and the match/team layer each
  hold their own piece from the start. **Map · City of Austin** and **Map · State of Texas** light nothing today,
  and that is the point: the cube shows the project's whole anatomy, dim where a piece is declared but not yet
  built, so building it lights a piece that already exists instead of re-cutting the cube.
- **DD-71 · R-CORE AND `</>` ARE TWO READINGS OF ONE COMPARE.** *"R-CORE shows revision differences and `</>`
  shows code for those revisions."* The prose history and the code are the same Before/After seen two ways —
  never two panels, never two screens.

### 15 — the chip's two sizes, verbatim

> Code must use smaller icon first and clicking opens larger code icon, after pressed code elements open

**Reading — DD-73.** Said after Verify Live #2828 put the Code mode on the site, so this is his hand on the live
chip, not a reaction to a picture. The SEQUENCE he names is already what ships (beat 1 the `</>` icon, beat 2 his
`</> Code` wordmark, beat 3 the code elements — DD-72 stands); what he is correcting is the **two sizes**.
Measured on the shipped build at 375 px, and against the real ink in his artwork (the neon halo means the box is
larger than the art — icon 55.5 % ink, wordmark 65.3 %): the resting icon was 31 × 20 with only ~11 px of visible
art, **larger than the 11 px labels beside it**, and the expanded wordmark 90 × 30 with ~20 px of art — a jump of
only 1.8×, so the press did not feel like it opened anything. **DD-73: smaller first, larger second** —
`CODE_CHIP_H` 20 → **14** (22 × 14, ~8 px of art, quieter than its neighbours) and `CODE_WORD_H` 30 → **42**,
which is `RCORE_LOGO_H` itself, the size the badge already gives his reticle and wordmark. The jump becomes 3.5×,
and binding the expanded state to R-CORE's own constant rather than a chosen number is what "work it just like
R-CORE" means at the level of the number and not only the grammar.

### Reading — DD-74 … DD-77

- **DD-74 · THE MOUSE CURSOR MOVES THE AIM, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ASK.** Three of the four behaviours he lists
  already exist and have since r.159/r.161 — the right click targets then approves (`pcTargetApprove`,
  play.html:4032, wired at :4177) and the left click fires a red mark under the bullseye (`mouseAim` :4027, wired
  at :4233). What the deck cannot do at all is **move the head with the mouse**: there is drag-look only
  (`:4208`, a button must be held) and no pointer lock anywhere in the file. His own follow-up says this plainly —
  *"the key for this update was using mouse cursor to move the target"* — so the revision is mouselook, and the
  two clicks are wiring already-built doors to it, not new behaviour.
- **DD-75 · IT IS BUILT FOR EVERY CRAFT, NOT FOR THE TURRET ALONE.** *"optimized mouse play for turret, that we
  can later apply to drone and robotics, as well as fixed wing and foil."* So the look is written against the
  same split `phys` already makes — the turret branch scaled by the picture's real half-angle (`:3085-3086`),
  every other craft at its own rate (`:3088-3089`) — and never hardcoded to `kind==='turret'`. A drone, a robot,
  a fixed wing or a foil seat inherits it by being seated, with no second control path to write.
- **DD-76 · INSIDE MOUSELOOK, LEFT ≡ F AND RIGHT ≡ SPACE.** His two answers reduce exactly to the two doors the
  deck already has, which is why the fire gate cannot drift: `pcTargetApprove` is the thing that refuses to fire
  and it is not touched, and `fireN` is the one gate that refuses anything not red and not under the pip.
- **DD-77 · HIS TWO ANSWERS COLLIDE ON ONE POINT, AND THE PLAIN CLICK WINS.** The mouselook answer's description
  said a click on the stage captures the cursor; his left-click answer says that **outside** mouselook a plain
  click keeps its present meaning. If a plain click captures, there is no outside left, and r.161's click-to-aim
  — *his own ask*: "click on mouse moves position of turret to center bulleye on target" — becomes unreachable on
  desktop for ever. Silently trading one of his shipped features for another is the class that cost three asks on
  the share sheet. **Decision, reversible in one word: the plain click keeps its r.161 meaning and the capture
  door is the `M` key**, announced by a one-time toast and a line in the CONTROLS panel beside the ones his
  screenshot already shows.

**Measured on the way and NAMED, not fixed:** `mouseAim`'s blank-ground branch (`:4031`) adds
`atan((sx−W/2)/f)` to `u.pan`, but `proj`/`camOf` make pan INCREASE mean look LEFT (confirmed by the shipped rows
`TURRET_LOOK_LEFT` :4357 and `PAN_RIGHT_MOVES_WORLD_LEFT` :4601), so a click on blank ground right of centre turns
the head AWAY from it — while the tilt term in the very same expression is correct. No gate sees it because
`MOUSE_CLICK_CENTRES_THE_BULLSEYE` asserts only that it turned, never the direction. It is r.161's behaviour and
his to call.

Sent after DD-80 shipped (`61ce2753a`, Verify Live #2855), so nine projects now carry the `</>` unlock and he has

Re-sent, the same two sentences without the battery line — which under his own law means the last answer missed:

> place code at bottom center of R-CORE JUST LIKE R-CORE IS AT BOTTOM center all screens
>
> align with supabase

Asked what "align with supabase" means, since there is **no Supabase element in any UI** (grep over `app/` and
`components/` finds it only as data plumbing, so there was nothing on screen to line the chip up with), he
answered:

> All hisyory needs to sit in sql wueries to supabase so we have history secured

### Reading — DD-81 and DD-82

- **DD-81 · THE `</>` CHIP TAKES THE BADGE'S OWN PLACEMENT, NOT JUST ITS GRAMMAR.** Today the chip is a third
  segment inside the WHAT CHANGED mode strip — a `role="group"` pill holding `cmpVU | cmpVS | cmpVC`
  (`components/2525-core/rcore-revision-panel.tsx:294-297`) — which is why it reads as an afterthought beside two
  text labels. "Just like R-CORE" is already a written law with a gate behind it: `rcore-badge.tsx:71` is
  `className="flex w-full justify-center"`, a **full-width centred row in normal flow, mounted last**, never a
  fixed overlay, and `tests/rcore-revisions.test.mjs:120` pins exactly that. The badge's own header comment
  carries his words of 2026-09-25: *"Smaller logo is in bottom center of all Innovation 2525 projects."* So DD-81
  is that same law **one level in**: the chip leaves the mode strip and becomes a full-width centred row at the
  **bottom of the panel body, mounted last**, in the badge's own class grammar. DD-72 already gave the chip the
  badge's two-stage language (icon → wordmark → open); this gives it the badge's place. The three beats are
  unchanged, and the code pane keeps its place in the body **above** the chip, so the chip stays put and the
  content changes above it — the badge's own behaviour when its panel opens.
- **DD-82 · ALL HISTORY SITS IN SQL QUERIES TO SUPABASE, SO THE HISTORY IS SECURED.** Every project's revision
  record and code record become rows in Supabase, queryable by SQL, in addition to the append-only ledgers in
  `docs/traceability/`.
- **ONE THING I OWE HIM PLAINLY, AND IT IS NOT A REFUSAL.** Supabase **on its own** is *less* durable than what
  the history has today, not more, and both numbers are already on this record. **DD-57, measured:**
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` / `_ANON_KEY` are **not set in GitHub Actions** (Deploy's `ship` job is skipped for
  exactly that reason) and **UNVERIFIED on Cloudflare**, the only real deployer — so if the panel read history
  *from* Supabase today, `supabase === null` would make **every history on every surface go blank**. And the
  redundancy stage recorded that this is a **single free-tier project with no replica** that **already paused
  once, on 2026-09-30**. Meanwhile the ledgers are append-only, hash-chained, gated by `traceability`,
  `drone-revisions` and `rcore-code-record`, and mirrored to every clone. So it is built as **two independent
  stores, which is his own doctrine in his own words — "Trinity everywhere"**: git stays the record of truth, and
  Supabase becomes the live, queryable copy, with the reader falling back to the generated module first so the
  panel is never blank. **Secured by two stores, not by moving from one to the other. One word and it becomes a
  move instead.**
- **WHAT ONLY HE CAN DO:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` as Actions secrets and
  in Cloudflare, plus a `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` for the writer. Until they exist the mirror cannot be written
  or proven from here, and that is reported as **UNVERIFIED**, never as done. The reader is built fallback-first
  precisely so nothing breaks while they are missing.
- **The battery line is not dropped.** *"AsM. MoTx Spiral, and ssses test overnight"* — the twelve Masters at 111
  words, the seven SSSES/Spiral lenses, three refuters per BLOCKING claim and MoT 333, plus the deterministic
  half (the 12-run SPIRAL soak, the full `test:ci`, the R-CORE history walk), run on whatever this stage ships and
  committed alone under `docs/assessments/`.

## Addendum (operator, 2026-10-17 — the 30-round magazine's rounds)

### 21 — his words, verbatim

Two phone screenshots of LIVE **r0.215**, both at MAG 2 · 30 RDS (persisted as `…_shot73.png` TRAIN DOWN, ALL
DOWN 0:33.0, HIT 11 · MISS 0, and `…_shot74.png` TRAIN UP, 250 M · PRESS TARGET, HIT 13 · MISS 0, the stop light
green on FIRE):

> Small fix for drone 2525; ensure 30 mags has rounds (visual projectiles that are top of magazine, not 70% of
> entire mag).

Also persisted with this entry, owed from the message before and referenced there but not copied at the time:
`…_shot70.png` (Security-2525's R-CORE scrolled to its foot), `…_shot71.png` (SoI-2525's compare, the `</>` still
inside the Unified / Side-by-side strip — the old bundle), `…_shot72.png` (SoI-2525 scrolled to its foot).

### Reading — DD-86, and the measured state of the drawing

- **DD-86 · A FULL MAGAZINE'S ROUNDS SIT AT THE TOP, AND A 30-ROUND MAGAZINE IS NOT A 70 % BAND.** He is
  describing the counter card at its fullest state and saying two things about it: the rounds belong at the
  **top** of the magazine, and what is drawn at 30 must not read as a band covering about seven tenths of the
  body.
- **MEASURED, before any change.** The counter's fill is the raster `docs/drone-2525/magazine/count-fill-*.png`,
  cut from **his own picture shot40** by `cut-magazine.py`; `BARS.json` records what was measured in that
  picture: **19 bars**, tops from **32.0 %** to **93.5 %** of the drawing, bottom **96.4 %**. The count is shown
  by clipping the fill from the top — `#magWell #magFill{clip-path:inset(var(--cut,32.0%) 0 0 0)}`
  (`play.html:36`) — with `--cut` set in `hudMag` (`play.html:971`):
  `cut = r >= 19 ? 32.0 % : (r <= 0 ? 96.4 % : MAG_BAR_TOPS[19 - r])`. So **at 30 rounds `--cut` is 32.0 %**, all
  nineteen bars show, and they occupy **32.0 % → 96.4 %, i.e. 64.4 % of the drawing's height, anchored at the
  bottom**, with the top third carrying the window and the neck. That is the "70 % of entire mag" he is pointing
  at, and the number is 64.4 %.
- **PHYSICALLY, HIS INSTINCT IS RIGHT.** A real magazine's follower presses the stack UP against the feed lips:
  rounds are consumed from the top but the remaining stack always touches the top, and the empty space opens at
  the BOTTOM. Today the clip removes from the top, so as rounds fall the surviving band sinks toward the floor of
  the magazine — the inverse of a real one.
- **TWO READINGS OF "visual projectiles that are top of magazine", and they are materially different work**, so
  they are put to him with rendered previews rather than guessed at: (A) **the stack is top-anchored and full at
  30** — the bars start at the feed lips and the gap grows downward as he fires; or (B) **the rounds are drawn as
  a few discrete projectiles near the top** — the visible tip of the stack rather than one bar per round down the
  body. (A) keeps his r.203 law of one bar per round; (B) replaces it.
- **THE HUD LAW GOVERNS THIS and points the same way.** *"THE RANGE'S HUD ROW IS THE OPERATOR'S OWN PICTURES,
  NEVER DRAWN … A new look for the row is a new picture from him, cut and pinned — never a drawing, never a
  guess."* The established way through that, set by r.203 itself, is that **the session renders candidates and he
  sends one back as the master** — shot40 is exactly that: "the r.200 preview … that he sent back as his
  reference". So previews are rendered, he picks, the pick is cut and pinned, and the gates
  (`tests/magazine-rasters.test.mjs`, `MAGAZINE.sha256`, `BARS.json`) move with it.

  headers and **no `Cache-Control` at all**. Revalidation was added twice before, each time for ONE file and each
  time AFTER he reported a stale page — `/drone-2525/play.html` (r.202: "his phone's tab held r0.198 while the site
  served r0.201") and `/whitepaper/vision-2525.html` (r143: "a push once read as shipped while the site served a
  four-hour-old copy"). The nine React R-CORE surfaces never got the line, so his phone held an old HTML document
  and rendered the previous build. **Third occurrence of one class; twice I fixed the instance.** His own
  screenshot proves it: the `</>` sits INSIDE the Unified / Side-by-side pill, which is the layout DD-81 removed.
- **A SECOND REASON, MEASURED:** the two rasters are **2.3 MB together**, so on cellular the chip is an empty dark
  rectangle for several seconds after the panel opens — another way he reaches the bottom and sees nothing.

### His three decisions (AskUserQuestion, 2026.10.17)

| # | asked | his answer |
|---|---|---|
| **D-24** | what ships first, given the cache finding and a live Supabase exposure on Architect-2525 | **"Cache fix, then Architect RLS"** — then the dotted dates, then the magazine |
| **D-25** | the magazine at 30 rounds, A1 (his pitch, 26 bars) or A2 (thirty bars) | **"A2 — thirty bars, one per round"** |
| **D-26** | his 2.3 MB rasters: serve as-is, or shrink | **"Shrink pixels only, never proportions"** — the byte-identical master stays pinned in `docs/asks/img/`; the served copy is a UNIFORM downscale, same aspect and ink ratio, fewer pixels |

- **D-25 in full:** thirty bars, one per round, from the top of the body to the floor; his bar height kept, the
  pitch closed from 6.50 px to 5.76 px; each bar leaves the frame's ink showing so the gold grid and the witness
  window still read through the gaps (his r.203 rule); and the clip inverts so the gap opens at the FLOOR as the
  follower rises, instead of the stack sinking. Measured on the shipped deck for comparison: at 30 rounds
  `--cut` is 32.0 %, all nineteen of his bars show, and they occupy 32.0 % → 96.4 % — **64.4 % of the drawing,
  anchored at the bottom**, with the top third bare.
- **D-26 is not the earlier mistake.** That one rendered his art tiny inside a 16 px box, destroying the ink
  ratio; this changes pixel count only, uniformly, and a gate asserts the served file's proportions equal the
  master's.

Sent after the three-up comparison (NOW · A1 · A2, each at 30 and at 15 rounds):

### Reading — DD-87, and it CORRECTS me

- **DD-87 · THE FILL IS PROPORTIONAL AND IT SITS ON THE FLOOR.** *"from there everything is proportional down to
  one at the bottom."* The fill height tracks the count — 30 of 30 fills the body, 15 of 30 fills half, 1 of 30
  is a single bar — and it is **anchored at the BOTTOM**, so the last round left is the one resting on the
  magazine's floor.

  deck reads like a gauge that empties downward, not like a cutaway of a real magazine. His picture, his call —
  the physics note is recorded once here and does not come back.

  what makes *"one at the bottom"* exact: with thirty bars, one round shows exactly one bar. A1's 26 bars at his
  pitch cannot land on a clean single bar at a count of one. So the build is **A2's thirty-bar raster**, filling
  to the top at 30 exactly as the top-middle card he called acceptable, emptying proportionally from the top
  down, anchored on the floor. If by "top middle" he meant A1's **density** rather than its fullness, that is

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.17 — the deck still has no code, and the audit that proves it)

### 24 — his words, verbatim

> check `</>` and `</> CODE` Iconology is now executible on on all R-CORE pages

and then, after the audit:

> still don't see access to code at bottom of Drone-2525

### Reading — he is right, and this is the THIRD time he has asked

- **THE DECK HAS NO CHIP AT ALL. Not hidden, not covered, absent.** Measured on the shipped
  `frontend/public/drone-2525/play.html` (1,195,795 B): `data-rcore-mode="cmpVC"` **0 hits**, `code-icon` **0**,
  `code-wordmark` **0**, `DRONE_2525_CODE` **0**, `rcore-code-row` **0**. Its panel is a hand-written vanilla
  port whose mode row is built as a string at `:6388-6392` with exactly two buttons (`cmpVU`, `cmpVS`); the
  render below it is a two-way if/else with no third arm, and the dispatcher at `:6612` can only ever set
  `unified` or `side`. This is **DD-79**, and the drone ledger's own r.216 entry already says *"It is next."* It
  has been deferred at every revision since r.196.
- **THE FULL AUDIT, driven headlessly at 428×926 — open the panel, press the chip through all three beats:**
  twelve `<RCoreBadge>` call sites, nine pass a code record, all nine records non-empty.
  - **Executable end to end (the code pane opens with real content, 675 → 107,612 chars):** Security-2525,
    Architect-2525, Celestial-2525, Financial-2525, SensorFusion-2525.
  - **Chip present, badge below the fold but reachable:** the Drone **React route** `/main/Drone-2525/`
    (94 revisions) — badge at y=2296 of a 2413 px page. Not the deck.
  - **BLOCKED — the badge renders and a tap never reaches it:** `/light-codex` (a `fixed` z-index 70 overlay,
    the cube modal) and `/encrypted-messaging` (`fixed` z-index 50, the composer).
  - **Gated:** `/SoI-2525` mounts the badge only after the unlock code.
  - **Correctly without, by decision:** `/vision-2525` (**DD-62**, his own words — a white paper has no code),
    `/sim` and the Settings drawer (surfaces, not projects).
  - So: **five fully executable, two blocked, one gated, three correctly without — and the one he plays on has
    nothing.**
- **WHY IT WAS DEFERRED, AND WHY THAT REASON HAS NOW EXPIRED.** The deck fetches nothing by law, so his two
  rasters would have to be inlined: **2,337,520 B raw ≈ 3,116,693 B as base64** — more than twice the whole
  deck, against the 7,770,000 B ceiling (`tests/drone-playable.test.mjs:13`). **D-26 removes the obstacle**: his
  own decision, *"shrink pixels only, never proportions"*, takes the pair to roughly 150 KB. So the raster
  shrink is not a parallel errand — it is the **prerequisite** that makes the deck's chip possible, and doing it
  first is what unblocks the thing he has asked for three times.
- **WHAT THE DECK'S CODE MODE WILL SHOW.** The index is 41,312 B and inlines; the patch bodies are ~4.2 MB and
  cannot. So the deck shows, per revision, the areas and files touched with their ± counts and the
  **carried-artefact provenance** — the patcher path, the carried file, the sha256, the chain hash. That is the
  deck's declared model and it is stronger than a diff: a patcher that refuses on one moved anchor is a better
  account of a change than a diff of the result. Where a body is not inlined the pane says so and names the repo
  path — never a blank pane.
- **DD-88 · A BADGE THAT CANNOT BE PRESSED IS NOT SHIPPED.** The two blocked surfaces were found only because a
  probe was run by hand. From now on a gate presses the badge on **every** project route and fails when the press
  does not open the panel, so "unreachable" can never again be something only one hand-run probe knows.
- **PERSISTED WITH THIS ENTRY, because they existed nowhere else:** `…_shot77_rcore-icon-master.png` (985,314 B)
  and `…_shot78_rcore-wordmark-master.png` (859,727 B) — the R-CORE badge's own two rasters. They had **no master
  in `docs/asks/img/` and no sha pin anywhere in the repo**, so shrinking them under D-26 would have destroyed
  the only copy. These are the served bytes promoted to master and hashed, stated plainly as such rather than
  presented as a fresh send from him.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.17 — a code section for EACH revision)

### 25 — his words, verbatim

> i asked for code section added for each revision (minimum have the latest code for the latest revision for
> each project
>
> the unlock is icon wand icon plus CODE visual (just like we did for R-CORE two step unlock).

### Reading — his minimum bar FAILS on six of nine, measured

- **THE BAR, AND WHERE IT STANDS.** Across 505 code rows in the nine projects that carry a record: **430
  `recorded`**, **45 `no-code-change`** (the commit genuinely altered nothing inside that project's own
  declared paths), **30 `no-commit` / `unreachable`**. The **newest row of six projects is among the 30** —
  Architect r4, Celestial r3, Security r4, SensorFusion r77, Light Codex r20 (all with an empty `commit` in
  the ledger) and SoI r131 (whose commit is the literal string `PENDING`). For those six no payload file
  exists under `frontend/public/r-core/code/<id>/` at all. Only **Drone r94, Financial r166 and Encrypted
  Messaging r6** meet his minimum today.
- **THE CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT CLERICAL, AND THAT MATTERS FOR THE FIX.** A record row is written *in* the
  commit that carries it, so at the moment of writing nobody can know the sha. The deck solves this with its
  two-commit chain and a records commit that fills `shipped` afterwards; the nine React ledgers have no such
  step, so **the newest row is permanently blank — and the newest row is the one he looks at first.**
- **IT IS FULLY RECOVERABLE.** The commit that introduced a row into its ledger is discoverable with
  `git log -1 -S'"rev": N' -- <ledger>`. Measured on all thirty: **30 resolve, 0 remain empty**, and each
  resolved commit carries a real scoped delta — Architect +8, Celestial +8, Security +9, SensorFusion +25,
  SoI +108 across four files, Light Codex +23. So the fix is to resolve it in the generator and record
  `commitSource`, which also makes it **self-healing**: a row appended today gets its code on the next
  generate, and nobody is ever asked to write a sha they cannot yet know.
- **"EACH REVISION" IS NOT WHAT THE VIEW DOES TODAY, and reading it found a second defect.**
  `rcore-code-view.tsx` is built on the compare RANGE: `crossed = code.revs.filter(r => r.r > fromRev && r.r
  <= toRev)` (`:32-35`); its only selection state is an **area** index (`:64`); and the patch it shows is
  `active.revs[active.revs.length - 1]` (`:92`) — the newest crossed revision that touched that area. So in a
  multi-revision window the chip reads `+add/−del` **summed over N revisions** while the `<pre>` shows the
  patch of exactly **one**, with no way to reach the other N−1. Tapping a row in the history list sets
  **After** (`panel:140-141`) and has nothing to do with the code view.
- **THE UNLOCK IS ALREADY HIS TWO-STEP, confirmed in code.** `codeStage: 0 | 1`, `pressCode` → beat 2 expands
  the `</>` icon to his `</> Code` wordmark, beat 3 opens the code elements — the same shape as the badge's
  `stage` / `expand` / `openPanel`. Three mechanical differences remain and are closed with D-28's work: the
  badge uses two elements where the chip branches inside one; the badge resets on Escape and on close while
  the chip resets only by pressing Unified or Side-by-side; and a third press on the chip is a silent no-op
  rather than a toggle back.

### His two decisions (AskUserQuestion, 2026.10.17)

| # | asked | his answer |
|---|---|---|
| **D-27** | what the 45 "changed nothing in this project" revisions should show | **"Show the project's code AS OF that revision"** — a snapshot, so nothing ever reads as empty |
| **D-28** | should tapping one revision show that revision's code | **"Yes — tap a revision, see that revision's code"** |

- **D-27's cost, measured, because the filter IS the design.** A naive snapshot is impossible: the nine
  projects' declared paths hold **112,431,083 B** at HEAD (Drone alone 49.8 MB, Security 38.7 MB) because
  those paths carry data blobs — DEM tiles, border JSON, the 1.2 MB deck. Filtered to **source extensions
  only, ≤ 64 KB per file, ≤ 256 KB per revision**, each project keeps 3–37 files at 17–262 KB. **45 rows ×
  ≤ 256 KB ≤ 11.5 MB**, lazily fetched, never bundled — the tier the existing ~14 MB of patch payloads already
  uses. Every skipped file is named with its reason; a snapshot that silently omits half a project is worse
  than none.

### Reading — two things, and the first one closes an argument I was having with myself

- **DD-79 IS CONFIRMED FROM HIS OWN GLASS, AND HE WALKED IT TO PROVE IT.** He did not just say the chip was
  missing — he photographed the badge, the opened panel and the panel's own foot, in order, so there is no
  reading of "you must not have scrolled". Measured in the shipped bytes to match: the deck carries
  `data-rcore-badge` 1 · `data-rcore-pill` 1 · `data-rcore-panel` 2 · `data-rcore-scrub` 1, and
  **`cmpVC` 0 · `code-icon` 0 · `code-wordmark` 0 · `codeStage` 0.** Nothing is hidden or covered. There is no
  chip there to reach. This is his **third** report of it.
- **AND IT CORRECTS SOMETHING I TOLD HIM AN HOUR EARLIER.** I reported the table as "seven of nine projects"
  and separately implied the deck panel's header would read `/drone-2525/play.html`. Both were loose.
  Drone-2525 is **one ledger with two carriers** — the React route and the deck — and the deck's panel header
  is built from the ledger's own `route` field, which is `/main/Drone-2525`; that is why shot80 reads
  `/main/Dr…` while he never left the deck. The honest count is **nine of ten carriers green, the deck the one
  gap.**
- **DD-89 · THE REVISION SCROLL CRASHES ON DRONE-2525 AND DOES NOT ON VISION-2525.** This is new, it is his
  own measurement on his own phone, and he has named the reference implementation himself: the Vision-2525
  white paper, *"style and memory methods"*. The two are genuinely comparable and that is what makes the ask
  precise — Vision-2525's living document carries **~311 revisions** over an append-only ledger with a
  `replay()` contract and scrolls them without failing, while the deck's panel carries **94** and fails. So
  the defect is not scale; it is method. The fix is to port Vision-2525's method rather than to invent one.
- **WHAT THE DECK'S PANEL IS, for the record:** a hand-written vanilla-JS port of the shared React panel,
  landed in r.210 (DD-49) because the deck is one static file and cannot import the React component. A port is
  exactly where a missing guard, a missing memo or a synchronous recompute hides — the shared panel and the
  port can look identical and behave differently under the thumb. Whether the same defect also sits in the
  shared React panel that the nine other surfaces use is being measured, not assumed.
- **ORDER, UNCHANGED BY THIS.** He has already ruled: the `</>` chip first (**r.216**), the thirty-bar
  magazine after (**r.217**). DD-89 lands with r.216 — the crash is in the very panel the chip is being added
  to, and shipping a new control into a panel that fails under the thumb would be adding a room to a house
  with a cracked floor.

### Reading — DD-91 · THE SPIRAL STARTS UPWARD, AND IT IS A ONE-CONSTANT CHANGE

- **HIS ASK IS "CONFIRM", SO IT WAS CONFIRMED BY COMPUTATION, NOT BY EYE.** All three of his grids are one
  contiguous clockwise spiral from the centre: **UP → RIGHT → DOWN → DOWN → LEFT → LEFT → UP → UP.** Every
  cell is visited exactly once; the pattern is identical on all three levels; the centres are 1 · 10 · 19 at
  (2,2,L), unchanged.
- **IT DIFFERS FROM WHAT THE REPO DECLARES TODAY, and that is the fix he is asking for.** The shipped spiral
  starts to the RIGHT of centre — `7 8 9 / 6 1 2 / 5 4 3` — declared at `frontend/lib/cube-grid.ts` as
  `SPIRAL_OFFSETS = [[6,7,8],[5,0,1],[4,3,2]]`, and written again in CLAUDE.md's 27-row coordinate table
  (Cube 2 = (2,3,1), Cube 9 = (1,3,1)), in its ASCII grids, and in `components/cube-status.tsx`.
- **THE VALUE THAT REPRODUCES ALL THREE OF HIS GRIDS EXACTLY is `[[8,1,2],[7,0,3],[6,5,4]]`** — verified
  arithmetically for every cube on every level. It is the current matrix **rotated 90° counter-clockwise**,
  which is the whole of the geometric change: nothing is renumbered, the layout is turned.
- **HIS REASON IS THE JOURNEY, AND IT IS WORTH RECORDING AS THE WHY:** *"user login in the middle, then
  spiraling around full prioritized survey then level 2 simulation and other functions build off first two
  in level 3."* The centre is where a user starts; the ring is the order in which they meet the product.
- **THE CARRIERS ARE KNOWN AND THE DUPLICATE IS THE HAZARD.** `cube-grid.ts` is the declared single source,
  but `components/cube-status.tsx` **does not import it** — it hard-codes three `CubeInfo[][]` arrays, held
  in step only by `tests/cube-grid.test.mjs`. That gate is **green on the old order**, so it must be
  re-expressed in the same commit and then shown to still be capable of failing. A full sweep for every
  other carrier — the CLAUDE.md table, every ASCII grid, the prose "spiral right, down, and up", the docs
  and the living document — is running before anything is edited.
- **HIS ORIGINAL 2026-09-15 ASK DECLARED THE OLD ORDER** (`docs/asks/2026-09-15_cube_coordinates.md`). Under
  the append-only law that file is history and is **never edited**; this addendum is the new decision and
  names it superseded.
- **ONE GRID THAT MUST NOT MOVE:** Cube 10's internal 3×3 (SIM · RPL · CHK / MTR · FB · VER / BCK · TRG ·
  APR) is a different diagram, not this spiral. It is explicitly out of scope and is being confirmed as such.

### Reading — DD-92 · THE LOOP HE IS DESCRIBING, recorded as direction rather than as a task

Four sentences that are not a feature request but the shape of the system, and they belong on the record
because they decide what later work is for:

1. **Cubes 1–27 and the Level-1 polling layer are the mechanism by which the platform updates itself**,
   semi-automatically, with **AI + HI** together. That is what the SIM's check-in → simulate → compare →
   decide engine and its three autonomy tiers (Manual ACTIVE · Semi-Auto · Autonomous) are for.
2. **Feedback from the embedded polling APIs is how Drone-2525 evolves** — the API-first/SDK model turned
   back on the product itself, so the Range's own users prioritise its backlog.
3. **The Divinity Guide's commentary of values supplies the S.I. input**, and only **if the admin activates
   it** — an opt-in, not a default.
4. ***"You have all the tools for AI + SI + HI system of tools to innovate at the speed of thought."***

Nothing is built from this addendum. It is persisted because it is the frame every later decision is judged
against, and because it is his voice.

- **D-29 in full.** Both are shared, so both are fixed in the deck's vanilla port **and** in
  `frontend/components/2525-core/rcore-revision-panel.tsx` in the same revision, so the port never diverges
  from the panel it is a port of. (a) A swipe that starts on the slider track both scrolls and jumps the
  revision — measured deck 92 → 47 while scrolling 303 px, React 93 → 47, i.e. native `<input type=range>`
  touchstart behaviour; the obvious `touch-action:pan-y` guard was tested and **does not work**, because the
  value is set at touchstart before a pan direction is known, so it needs a pointerdown guard. (b) Press
  ADDED, scrub to a window where `added === 0`: the chip disappears, `RCP.filter` stays `'added'`, the
  summary reads "Filtered: added", the table reads "—", and there is no control left to clear it.
  `rcore-revision-panel.tsx:119-120` resets `navIdx` on a scrub and never the stat filter — the same hole.
- **D-30 in full.** Measured, not estimated: the deck is 1,195,795 B against a 7,770,000 B ceiling, and the
  93 payloads total 4,868,652 B (mean 52,351 B, max 230,076 B). **N=10 → 2,124,310 B, 27.3 % of the ceiling,
  5,645,690 B left.** Revisions outside the window say so by name and point at the React route, which can
  fetch. The reason this was his and not mine: at all 93 the deck keeps only 1.6 MB, which is about thirty
  more revisions of patch and no room for the next picture he sends — headroom on this deck is his picture
  budget. **The chosen N is stated in the register row**, so a later reader knows why the window ends.
- **D-31 in full.** `CubeVoxel3D` was already lifted out of `cube-dev-sim.tsx` into
  `components/2525-core/cube-voxel-3d.tsx` for exactly this reuse, but the deck has no React and no 3D
  library, so this is a from-scratch CSS-3D port: ~130 lines with drag-orbit, ~300 for full pinch and Explode
  parity, 189 DOM nodes, built on first Code open and torn down on panel close so it never sits over the
  running canvas loop uninvited. Verified before accepting the work: Drone's **15 declared areas partition
  all 27 voxel cells exactly** (27 used, 27 distinct, none twice, none spare), and the partition seed is the
  literal string `drone-2525` — `/drone-2525` and `drone` both produce different shapes, so the seed is
  pinned by a parity gate or the same project shows two anatomies on two carriers.
- **ALSO SETTLED BY D-31, and it is a gap in the React surfaces rather than new work:** `CubeVoxel3D`
  implements the whole Explode branch and declares `exploded = false`, but `rcore-code-view.tsx:126-127`
  never passes the prop. His Explode button is **one withheld prop on nine live surfaces**, and it is closed
  in the same revision.

### A finding about his own SIM screenshots that he is owed plainly

He sent the SSSES tiles (`300 rows · 37 ms · 34 LOC`, SEC 96 · STAB 84 · SCAL 80 · EFF 99 · SUCC 98) as part
of the reference. **Those numbers are not measurements.** `frontend/lib/api.ts:10` reads
`const MOCK_MODE = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_MOCK_MODE !== "false"`, and nothing in the repo sets it false for
a deploy — only a local dev script's instructions — so the live page he photographed is answered by
`mock-data.ts`, where `:1022` is literally `const dur = 30 + fns.length * 7, rows = 300;` and `:1031`
hardcodes `measured: true`. His "37 ms" is `30 + 1 × 7`; his "300 rows" is the literal; SEC/STAB/SCAL are
flat formula defaults from keyword tests against Python function names. Only SUCC (from real LOC) and EFF
(from those two constants) move at all. This is recorded because it decides something: the deck's Code pane
does **not** copy those tiles, and the reason is not "the deck lacks the data" but "the reference numbers are
a formula wearing `measured: true`". The deck's own code already keeps a better rule — `play.html:3050-3052`
returns `eff: measured ? … : null` and `fpsStatus: measured ? 'MEASURED' : 'UNMEASURED'`. Whether the SIM's
own tiles should say so is his call and is not touched here.

## Addendum (operator decisions, 2026.10.18 — D-32 · D-33 · D-34, via AskUserQuestion)

| # | asked | his answer, verbatim |
|---|---|---|
| **D-32** | The SIM easter-egg picker renders LEVEL 1 ONLY. You drew all three grids — where should Levels 2 and 3 appear? | **"Add Level 2 / 3 tabs to the SIM"** |
| **D-33** | The .xlsx is the CRS matrix master and ten labels carry the old coordinates. Drop the suffix, update both, or update the .ts only? | **"1,1,1, is x y z keep, and 2,2,1 is position 1 on level 1; keep both"** |
| **D-34** | Drone declares 15 areas, so the newest revision lights only 2 of 27 cells. Split, ship as-is, or you draw the anatomy? | **"1-27 gives ya freedome; if drone-2525 has 2 sections you can do 3x3 level 1 visual with 3x3 level 2 with 3x3 level 3 stacked ; bascially ; first decide how many key sections of code there are then find visual way to represent"** |

- **D-32.** The easter-egg picker gains the same three level tabs Settings → Cube Architecture already has.
  Measured consequence to handle honestly: `cube-dev-sim.tsx` hard-codes a "Level 1" chip and calls
  `cubeGridOrder(1)`, and the LIVE-code blocks behind it exist only for cubes 1–9 — cubes 10–27 have no
  backend harness. So the Level 2 and 3 tabs must **say that plainly** rather than render an empty cube that
  looks broken. Also carried into that revision: a pre-existing defect on the very surface he named —
  `cube-dev-sim.tsx:281` filters AFTER ordering, so a missing cube shifts the survivors LEFT into the wrong
  cells and the comment's promise of "the cube's REAL positions" silently fails.
- **D-33 — he corrected a premise of mine, and I had recommended the wrong thing.** I proposed dropping the
  coordinate suffix from the CRS labels so the matrix would stop being a position carrier. He refused, and
  named why: **the two triples are two different readings and both are wanted** — `(1,1,1)` read as **x y z**,
  and `(2,2,1)` read as **position 1 on level 1**. So the suffix STAYS. Measured for the record: the matrix's
  `"cube"` field carries `Cube N — Name (row,col,level)` with the OLD geometry — 14 occurrences of (2,2,2)
  for Cube 10 and 4 of (2,2,1) for Cube 1 are the two **centres and do not move**; the other eight cubes carry
  two occurrences each, 16 in all, and those name cells that the restoration moves. **My reading of "keep
  both", stated so he can turn it in one word:** keep the suffix and keep both readings available; the values
  move with the cell, because the measurement shows the triple IS the grid cell. If he means the x-y-z
  numbers are a separate axis system that does NOT rotate, that is one sentence from him and the 16 stay.
- **D-34 — this is the instruction, and it replaces the question I asked.** He did not pick from my three
  options; he gave a method and freed the number. **"1-27 gives ya freedome"** — the three stacked 3×3 levels
  are the CANVAS, not a quota: a project with few sections uses one level, a richer one stacks two or three.
  **"first decide how many key sections of code there are then find visual way to represent"** is the order of
  work, and it supersedes the registry's fifteen areas, which were a prior guess and are now a hypothesis to
  test rather than a baseline to defend. An analysis is running against the evidence — the deck's ~557
  top-level declarations grouped by what the code actually does, the 52 refuse-on-missed-anchor patchers as
  the best record of what each revision really touched, and the 94-entry histogram of where work has actually
  landed — with two adversarial refuters on it: one testing that every declaration and file falls in exactly
  one section with the smallest honest "everything else", and one judging whether a typical revision lights a
  meaningful, varying set of cells and whether the names are **in his language** (the range, the targets, the
  magazine, the stop light, the Capitol, the crew link, the record) rather than an engineer's.

> lego is default, of touching sides so pieces combined form total 3x3x3 cube

…followed by the full original CRS user-story table, carried verbatim as
`docs/asks/2026.10.18_13.13..36_original_crs_cube_1_27.md`.

### Reading — DD-94 · THE COORDINATES WERE NEVER WRONG. THE REPO'S READING OF THEM IS.

**This stops a change that would have corrupted correct data.** One message earlier I had an 88-edit plan
that rewrote CLAUDE.md's 27 coordinate rows, 16 CRS matrix labels, `innovation-data.ts`, `Cube_Flow_Table.md`,
ten `Requirements.txt` headings and a tracked `.xlsx`. **Every one of those is already right.**

The tell is his own word. *"go **up** to 2,3,1"* — from (2,2) to (2,3) is only "up" if the second field is a
**y measured upward**, not a column. Tested both readings against the grid he drew in the previous message:

| the triple read as | level 1 renders | matches his drawn grid |
|---|---|---|
| `(row, col, level)`, row 1 at top — **the repo's reading** | `7 8 9 / 6 1 2 / 5 4 3` | **NO** |
| `(x, y, level)`, y upward — **his reading** | `9 2 3 / 8 1 4 / 7 6 5` | **YES** |

His nine-step walk expressed in (x,y) reads **UP → RIGHT → DOWN → DOWN → LEFT → LEFT → UP → UP**, which is
verbatim what he wrote. Checked against every carrier, by computation:

- **CLAUDE.md's 27-row table** — all three levels reproduce his grids exactly under (x,y,level). No edit.
- **`frontend/lib/crs-matrix-data.ts`** — all ten cubes reproduce his level 1 under (x,y,level). No edit.
- **The original CRS table he pasted** — `Cube 1 (1,2,2)`, `Cube 2 (1,2,3)`, `Cube 3 (1,3,3)` … uses
  **(level, x, y)**, the same geometry with level first, and reproduces his grid exactly. No edit.
- `innovation-data.ts`, `Requirements.txt`, `Cube_Flow_Table.md`, `docs/CUBES_7-9.md`,
  `docs/CUBE_19_27_LEVEL_3_FRAMEWORK.md` and the original whitepaper SVG — all consistent with his geometry.
  **Verify-only, diffed to zero.**

**So D-33 was him telling me exactly this and I only half-heard it.** *"1,1,1, is x y z keep"* names the
FIELD ORDER — x·y·z — not a second coordinate system living beside a row/col one. Recorded as a correction to
my own D-33 reading, which said "the values move with the cell". **They do not move. Nothing moves.**

**What is actually defective** is one renderer and one sentence: commit `1912f0fa1` (2026-09-15 15:49)
re-read the triple as `(row, col, level)` — its own message admits the convention had been *"(level, row,
col) with the spiral starting at the TOP"* — and `20011e5d2` built `lib/cube-grid.ts` on that reading three
minutes later. The change is `SPIRAL_OFFSETS`, `cubeCoord()`'s return shape, the Settings map's three arrays
(reordered, never renumbered), the ASCII art, the convention sentence, and the gate. **Not one coordinate.**

### Reading — DD-93 · LEGO IS THE DEFAULT, and it is a geometric constraint on the Code cube

*"lego is default, of touching sides so pieces combined form total 3x3x3 cube."* This binds the `</>` Code
pane's anatomy, and it arrived after the section analysis was launched, so it is applied at the end rather
than assumed. Three properties, each gated:

1. **Touching sides** — every section's cells form a **face-adjacent polycube**, not scattered singletons.
2. **The pieces combine into the whole** — the sections **tile all 27 cells**, none spare, none shared.
3. Every section is declared even when 0 of 94 revisions touched it, and shows visibly dim rather than hidden.

This is why his SIM reads as Lego blocks and the current arrangement does not: today's cells come from a
**seeded** partition, which scatters singletons and pairs at arbitrary positions. A seeded partition cannot
satisfy "touching sides", so the partition must be **built** and gated on connectivity — which also settles
the open question of whether the sections should be split: the count and the shapes now have to satisfy a
geometric law, not only a taxonomic one.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.18 — DD-95, the canonical field order)

> ah thats right:  Level, X, Y

### Reading — the geometry was never the problem, and now the ORDER is settled too

His four words name the canonical field order: **(Level, X, Y)** — level first, then X across, then Y
upward. Measured against every carrier, by computation, testing both orders and requiring the level field to
agree with the cube's own number:

| carrier | field order | geometry | action |
|---|---|---|---|
| **his CRS table** (the reference he just sent) | **(Level, X, Y)** | correct | the canon |
| `Requirements.txt` — `Cube 1 (1,2,2)` … `Cube 10 (2,2,2)` | **(Level, X, Y)** | correct | **no edit** |
| `docs/CUBES_7-9.md` — `(1,1,1)` bottom-left, `(1,1,2)` left, `(1,1,3)` top-left | **(Level, X, Y)** | correct, prose included | **no edit** |
| `docs/CUBE_19_27_LEVEL_3_FRAMEWORK.md` — `Cube 19 (3,2,2)` | **(Level, X, Y)** | correct | **no edit** |
| `CLAUDE.md` 27-row table — `Cube 1 (2,2,1)` | (X, Y, Level) | correct | **reverse the triple** |
| `Cube_Flow_Table.md` — `Cube 1 (2,2,1)` | (X, Y, Level) | correct | **reverse the triple** |
| `frontend/lib/crs-matrix-data.ts` | (X, Y, Level) | correct | **reverse the triple** |
| `frontend/lib/innovation-data.ts` | no coordinate triples found at all | — | nothing (the earlier sweep's claim was wrong) |

- **MOST OF THE REPO WAS ALREADY IN HIS CANONICAL ORDER.** Requirements.txt, the Level-3 framework and
  CUBES_7-9 — including its prose descriptors — have carried (Level, X, Y) the whole time. The three
  reversed carriers are the outliers, not the standard.
- **THE NORMALISATION IS MECHANICAL AND CHANGES NO GEOMETRY.** Reversing `(x,y,l)` to `(l,x,y)` moves no cube
  and renames no cell; it makes three files speak the same order as the other four and as him. Every reversed
  value is checkable by a single rule, which is what makes it safe where the earlier 88-edit geometry rewrite
  would not have been.
- **WHAT REMAINS A REAL DEFECT is still only the renderer:** `frontend/lib/cube-grid.ts`'s `SPIRAL_OFFSETS`
  and `cubeCoord()`'s return shape, and `frontend/components/cube-status.tsx`'s three hard-coded arrays,
  which between them draw `7 8 9 / 6 1 2 / 5 4 3` instead of his `9 2 3 / 8 1 4 / 7 6 5`. Plus the stated
  convention, which CLAUDE.md declares as "(row, col, level)" and which is **(Level, X, Y)**.
- **DD-94 STANDS, SHARPENED.** No cube moves. What changes is the order three files write the triple in, the
  renderer that reads it, and the sentence that names the convention.
