## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.18 14:41 CST — DD-97, efficiency is a smaller block in the same housing)

One screenshot of the SIM's parity panel, carried as `…_shot90.png`: `PARITY · +0% efficiency`, the line
*"beat LIVE by ≥11.1% to earn a smaller cube (8×8×8 for 10×10×10)"*, two voxel renders captioned
`LIVE · 100%` and `YOURS · 100%`, then `HOLD · manual — awaiting human approval`, the Pass / Revise / Block
row and **Submit contribution**.

> for the SIM, what i mean is smaller block goes in that space (still in housing), using a separate visual.
> Better metrics with same or better functionality visually show up as a smaller block or set of blocks.
> 8x8x8 10x10x10 was never mentioned by H.I.

### Reading — DD-97, and the first thing to say is that the bad copy is mine

- **"8x8x8 10x10x10 WAS NEVER MENTIONED BY H.I." IS CORRECT, AND I WROTE IT.** Traced: the string
  `beat LIVE by ≥${o.threshold_pct ?? 10}% to earn a smaller cube (8×8×8 for 10×10×10)` is
  `frontend/components/cube-dev-sim.tsx:533`, and the comment above it at `:520` repeats the invention —
  *"the candidate renders that % SMALLER than Live (8×8×8 replaces 10×10×10)"*. `git log -S'8×8×8'` returns
  **one commit: `06f2a2251`, 2026-07-24, author Claude.** Not Grok, not him — me. It is a number I made up
  to explain a visual, and it then taught the reader a rule he never set. It goes, and nothing replaces it
  but his own sentence.
- **THE HOUSING IS FIXED; THE BLOCK INSIDE IT SHRINKS.** *"smaller block goes in that space (still in
  housing)"* — the cell, the slot, the lattice position stays exactly the same size. What changes is how
  much of that housing the implementation occupies. **Efficiency is visible as space given back**, which is
  only legible if the space itself does not move. That is the opposite of what ships today: `:541` wraps
  the whole candidate render in `transform: scale(${o.cube_scale})`, so **both cubes shrink or grow as
  wholes** and there is no housing left to measure against — a reader sees two pictures of different sizes,
  not one job done in less room.
- **"USING A SEPARATE VISUAL"** — the smaller block is drawn in its own right, distinguishable from the LIVE
  block, not the same mesh at a smaller zoom. Today both panes call `CubeVoxel3D` with identical arguments
  and differ only by a CSS transform, which is exactly the thing he is saying is wrong.
- **"A SMALLER BLOCK OR SET OF BLOCKS"** — the result of a better implementation may be ONE smaller block or
  SEVERAL smaller ones. So the visual must express a decomposition, not only a scale factor: a cell's work
  can end up as two or three small pieces inside the same housing.
- **THIS IS THE SAME GRAMMAR AS DD-93 AND IT COMPLETES IT.** DD-93 said *"lego is default, of touching sides
  so pieces combined form total 3x3x3 cube."* DD-97 says what happens when a piece gets better: the housing
  it was given stays, and the piece needing less of it is the whole point. Together they make the cube a
  measure rather than an ornament — the lattice is the budget, a piece is a claim on it, and efficiency is
  returning part of the claim.
- **WHAT MUST NOT CHANGE WITH IT, so nobody tidies it in the same pass:** the verdict row
  (`HOLD · manual — awaiting human approval`), the `same output / faster / metrics-pass / replay` line, the
  `웃 outcome validators 0/3` rule that HI finalises only after ≥3 members validate, the Pass / Revise /
  Block controls and **Submit contribution**. He photographed them and said nothing about them.
- **ONE THING I WILL NOT GUESS, and will put to him rather than invent a second number:** what the threshold
  should be, now that `11.1%` is exposed as scaffolding around an invented example. It is live in
  `frontend/lib/mock-data.ts:1130` as `threshold_pct: 11.1` with `win: passed && improvement >= 0.111`.
  Whether a block shrinks **continuously** with the measured gain, or only once a declared bar is cleared,
  is his call — and after this correction I am not going to pick a figure and write it on the glass again.

### Reading — DD-98

- **THE DOTS GO.** `frontend/components/cube-status.tsx:129-139` renders a 3×3 grid of 3 px squares on
  every cube tile, fed by `cubeFingerprint()` in `frontend/lib/voxel-fingerprint.ts` — a seeded FNV-1a bit
  pattern, nine squares per tile, one lit/unlit per bit. That block is deleted.
- **THE HELPER'S OWN HEADER CREDITS THE DOTS TO HIM, AND IT SHOULD NOT.** `voxel-fingerprint.ts:5` reads
  *"the at-a-glance tile identity so no two cubes look alike (the operator's \"unique visual\")"*. He has
  just said he never asked for it. **This is the second attribution of a decision of mine to him found in
  one hour** — the first was `8×8×8 for 10×10×10` in `cube-dev-sim.tsx:533`, traced by `git log -S` to my
  own commit `06f2a2251` (DD-97). Both are the same failure: a choice I made, written into the repo as
  his. A false attribution in a comment is how an invented rule survives review, so it is recorded here
  rather than quietly rewritten.
- **WHAT STAYS, because he did not ask for it to go and release rule 6 binds:** the four completion
  segments directly beneath the dots (`progressSegments(cube.completion, 4)` at `cube-status.tsx:146`,
  which pair with the *"Deployed — 98%"* text visible in his own screenshot), the status dot in the tile
  corner, the cube number, the cube name and the description.
- **THE HELPER IS RETIRED, NOT KEPT FOR LATER.** `cubeFingerprint` has exactly two consumers in the whole
  repository — the block being deleted and its own 34-line test. It is also a *seeded* pattern, which is
  precisely what **DD-93** (*"lego is default, of touching sides so pieces combined form total 3x3x3
  cube"*) rules out, so it is not a primitive the Code-pane anatomy can reuse. `progressSegments()` in the
  same module stays, because it still feeds the completion bars. Reversible in one word if he wants the
  function kept.
- **THE GATE.** `frontend/tests/cube-grid.test.mjs` already reads `cube-status.tsx` and does not currently
  look at the dots at all — which is why nine tiles could carry an unasked-for visual with every gate
  green. It gains one assertion: no per-cube dot grid renders (`data-cube-fingerprint` absent,
  `cubeFingerprint` not imported) while `progressSegments` is still used. Shown RED on today's bytes first.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.18 14:38 CST — DD-99, the full-screen cube must prove its 3×3×3 is whole)

> Also full screen should have option to highlight 9.1-all sub blocks to make sure all 3x3x3 is not missing

Sent with a phone screenshot of the LIVE full-screen cube (persisted as
`docs/asks/img/2026.10.11_18.56..53_drone2525_shot91.png`, 380,425 B): SIMULATION MODE, the header
**`CUBE 9 · Reports & Dashboards · 8 BLOCKS`**, the Explode control, *"drag to rotate · pinch/scroll to
zoom"*, the × close, the A.I. / pause / S.I. row beneath. In the picture **three cells are lit solid blue**
and the rest of the lattice is a faint outlined grid — most of the 3×3×3 reads as empty.

### Reading — DD-99

- **"9.1-all" is the control he is naming:** a way to step the highlight through **each** sub-block of the
  cube he is looking at — `9.1`, `9.2`, … — **plus an ALL position that lights every one at once.** The
  section numbering is the cube's own (`<cube>.<block>`), which is the same numbering his two SIM examples
  used (Cube 1 · 1.1 and 1.4, Cube 2 · 2.1 — DD-90, as corrected: 1–27 is the CUBE across the three levels,
  the number after the dot is the block).
- **THE PURPOSE IS VERIFICATION, NOT DECORATION — "to make sure all 3x3x3 is not missing".** With ALL
  selected, every one of the 27 cells should be accounted for by some sub-block. **A cell that stays dark
  under ALL is a hole**, and the control's job is to make that hole visible on the glass instead of leaving
  it to be discovered later. That is the opposite of a highlight added for looks.
- **THIS IS DD-93 MADE CHECKABLE.** DD-93 said *"lego is default, of touching sides so pieces combined form
  total 3x3x3 cube."* DD-99 is the instrument that proves it: step through the pieces one at a time to see
  each piece's shape, then light them all to see whether the pieces together fill the cube. Without it the
  tiling rule is a claim; with it, it is something he can check in two taps.
- **HIS SCREENSHOT IS ALREADY THE EVIDENCE.** The header says **8 BLOCKS** and the picture shows three lit
  cells over a mostly empty lattice. Whatever the reason — a per-block highlight the full-screen view does
  not aggregate, blocks whose cells are not declared, or a partition that genuinely does not cover 27 — the
  thing he is asking for is the thing that would have told us. The measurement comes before any build, and
  **if the cube really is short of 27 cells that is reported as a finding in its own right, not silently
  filled in.**
- **SCOPE — the FULL-SCREEN view.** He said "full screen", which is the expanded voxel modal in his
  screenshot, not the small inline cube. Whether the inline cube gets the same control is a separate call
  and is not assumed here.
- **NOT DECIDED, and listed rather than guessed:** whether the highlight STEPS (one block at a time, with
  ALL as a position on the same control) or TOGGLES (each block independently on/off, with ALL as a
  shortcut); and whether an uncovered cell under ALL is drawn as a visible hole, flagged in words, or both.
  Both readings serve his sentence; the step form is the smaller change and the one the SIM's existing
  section-chip strip already implies.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.18 — DD-100, a revision belongs to its own section; and the 48-agent fleet)

> remember revisions are for that secrion onky.
>
> Drone-2525 maybe be on 218 but Architrct maybe be on its own revision
>
> same with eXeL Polling

> i did bot answer last set; ask again

> Master of Thought — activate full 48-agent fleet now.
>
> Structure:
> - 12 Master of Thought (MoT) agents
> - 36 specialist agents organized as 3 agents per AsM
> - Grouped into fleet pods of 4 AsMs each (3 × 4 = 12 agents per pod)
>
> For every AsM:
> - Spin up exactly 3 agents
> - All 3 generate independent work/feedback
> - Designate one of the three as Voting & Summarizer agent
> - That agent collects the other two agents’ outputs, votes, synthesizes, and produces the single final AsM feedback package
>
> 12 MoTs operate in parallel as senior coordinators across the fleet pods.
> All 48 agents are live and ready.
> Begin processing existing backlog under this exact hierarchy.
> Report only fleet readiness confirmation and any residual gaps.

### Reading — DD-100 · ONE COUNTER PER SECTION, NEVER A HOUSE NUMBER

- **EVERY PROJECT CARRIES ITS OWN REVISION NUMBER, AND THEY ARE NOT COMPARABLE.** Drone-2525 being at r.218
  says nothing about Architect-2525, which is on its own count, and nothing about eXeL AI Polling, which is on
  its own. There is no global revision for the platform and nothing may imply one.
- **WHAT THIS BINDS.** (a) A release note, a commit message or a chat report names the revision **with its
  section** — "Drone-2525 r.218", never a bare "r.218" that reads as the house's. (b) A revision number is
  **never carried across** from one project's ledger to another's, and a gate that compares two sections'
  numbers is comparing two different scales. (c) When several sections ship in one session, each is reported
  on its own counter; the session is not a revision.
- **WHY HE IS SAYING IT NOW, and what must be measured before anything is claimed:** the ten per-project
  ledgers are separate files with separate counters, so the DATA is already per-section. What is not yet
  measured is whether any SURFACE or any sentence of mine reads as a single house revision — the R-CORE panel
  headers, the registry, a release note, the Settings map. **That measurement is part of the fleet's backlog
  and the answer is reported as found, not assumed to be clean.**
- **"same with eXeL Polling"** — the polling app is itself one of the sections, with its own count. It is not
  the parent whose number the others inherit.

### Reading — the fleet directive

- **EXACT HIERARCHY, TAKEN LITERALLY.** 12 AsMs × 3 agents = **36 specialists**; the 12 AsMs grouped into
  **3 pods of 4** (12 agents per pod); **12 MoT** senior coordinators in parallel across the pods — **48
  agents**. Within each AsM all three produce independent work, and the third is additionally the **Voting &
  Summarizer**: it forms its own view first, then collects the other two, votes, and emits the single AsM
  package.
- **THE CHAIR IS THIS SESSION.** He addressed the directive to Master of Thought, so the final report is
  mine, not a 49th agent's.
- **"Report only fleet readiness confirmation and any residual gaps"** — the reply is short: the fleet is up,
  and what is still missing. Not a retelling of the backlog.
- **"i did bot answer last set; ask again"** — the open decisions are re-put to him as questions rather than
  decided for him. They are, at this moment: the shape of DD-99's highlight control and how a hole is shown;
  the DD-97 parity threshold, which I said after the `8×8×8` correction I would not pick a second time; and
  what ships next behind DD-98.

## Addendum (operator decisions, 2026.10.18 — D-38 · D-39 · D-40 · D-41, via AskUserQuestion)

He had said *"i did bot answer last set; ask again"*, so the open decisions were re-put rather than taken.
Four answers, verbatim where he typed his own words.

### D-38 · DD-99's highlight STEPS, with ALL at the end

> Step through, ALL at the end

One control, tapped: **9.1 → 9.2 → … → 9.N → ALL**, then round again. Not independent toggles, not an
auto-cycle. This is also the smallest change and it matches the numbered section-chip strip the SIM already
has, so nothing new is invented.

### D-39 · THERE IS NEVER A HOLE — the pieces always fill all 27, and the shapes are mine to decide

Asked how an uncovered cell should show itself, he **rejected the premise of the question** and answered with
the rule instead:

> the 3x blocks should always cover all 27 blocks, just like legos; shapes and now 3 sections cover 27 sub
> cubes within larger blocks is many (you must decide and ensure all 27 are filled each time

- **THE PARTITION IS TOTAL, ALWAYS.** Whatever a project's section count, the pieces together cover **all 27
  cells, every time**. A hole is therefore **not a display state to design — it is a build error.** The
  question I asked was the wrong question, and his answer is the better one.
- **THE SHAPES VARY, AND THEY ARE MINE TO CHOOSE.** *"you must decide"* — the shape of each lego piece is my
  decision, under DD-93's standing rule that pieces touch on their sides. He is not going to specify them.
- **FEW SECTIONS MEANS BIG PIECES.** *"now 3 sections cover 27 sub cubes within larger blocks is many"* — with
  three sections, each piece is a large block of **nine** sub-cubes; with nine sections, three each; with
  twenty-seven, one each. The count of pieces changes their size, never the total.
- **SO THE CONTROL'S JOB CHANGES.** Stepping 9.1 … ALL is **confirmation that the tiling is whole**, and the
  gate behind it must make a short partition impossible rather than merely visible. `9.1 … ALL` showing 27 of
  27 is the normal reading; anything else is a defect that should have failed a gate before it reached glass.
- **AND IT SETTLES THE OPEN QUESTION FROM DD-93.** The 27-section measurement found
  `orderedPartition("drone-2525", 27)` returns the identity — the only count at which geometry can be chosen
  rather than seeded. D-39 says the choosing is mine at **every** count, not only at 27.

### D-40 · DD-97's parity is a SEPARATE VIEW, scaled by the % gain — no threshold

> A separatr view that shows that section smaller by % efficientcy gain

- **Continuous, not a bar.** The section is drawn **smaller in proportion to the measured % efficiency gain**.
  There is no declared threshold to clear, so the live `threshold_pct: 11.1` at `frontend/lib/mock-data.ts:1130`
  is scaffolding with nothing left to hold up.
- **A SEPARATE VIEW, which is his word twice now** — DD-97 already said *"using a separate visual"*. The
  smaller block is its own drawing of **that section**, not the whole candidate cube scaled by CSS. That
  retires `cube-dev-sim.tsx:541`'s `transform: scale(o.cube_scale)` on the whole pane.
- **Scope is the SECTION, not the cube.** He wrote *"that section smaller"* — the thing that shrinks is the
  piece whose implementation improved, inside the housing DD-97 fixed.
- The invented `8×8×8 for 10×10×10` copy at `:533` goes with it, as DD-97 already required.

### D-41 · Drone-2525 r.217 ships next — the thirty-bar magazine

> Drone-2525 r.217 — the thirty-bar magazine (Recommended)

Built, rendered at six round counts, and already approved by him (*"option 2 is correct"*). It ships behind
DD-98. Named here **with its section**, per DD-100.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-101, the revert class: "as you sometimes due")

> well done on SIM; keep it up for Drone-2525

> remember We had this addressed in past revisions but some reason you reverted; as you sometimes due

### Reading — DD-101

- **THE SECOND SENTENCE IS A STANDING FINDING ABOUT ME, NOT A ONE-OFF.** *"as you sometimes due"* names a
  **class**: work that was already addressed in a past Drone-2525 revision and later came undone in one of
  mine. Under FIX THE CLASS the answer is not to patch whichever instance he has in mind — it is to find
  **every** behaviour this has happened to, and to put a gate under the class so a restore can never again
  silently drop work that was not part of what it was restoring.
- **I DO NOT KNOW WHICH "THIS" HE MEANS, AND I AM NOT GOING TO GUESS.** Guessing is how the share sheet cost
  him three asks. It is recorded here as open, and a measurement across every carried revision, every
  patcher and the ledger is what names it — not my recollection.
- **THE CLASS IS REAL AND ALREADY ON THE RECORD TWICE, in my own commits:**
  - `b2dfa80a` *"Reinstate the r.166 range"* restored exact r0.166 bytes and **dropped r.167–r.176 in one
    move (−2,450 lines)**, including the r.169 fix that put every QUAL target behind TARGET → APPROVE →
    FIRE. He had to report that behaviour again.
  - r.211's share sheet: *"I still do not see why you regress; use image 2 formate for the range"* — a
    restore six revisions earlier threw away work that was not part of what it was restoring, **and no gate
    measured what the sheet looked like**, so six revisions shipped over a broken screen with everything
    green.
  - The magazine he has now reported **three** times: *"this is not a full visual of 30 round magazine;
    **this was right a few days ago**; fix"* — his own words already said it had been right before.
- **THE COMMON MECHANISM, stated so the gate can be aimed at it:** a restore or a re-cut takes a known-good
  base and re-applies only the changes it is thinking about. Anything added between that base and HEAD that
  nobody listed is gone, and **no gate notices, because the gates that exist guard the thing being
  restored**. A revision is proven by what it changes and never by what it must not lose.
- **FIRST SENTENCE: the SIM standard is approved and is now the bar for Drone-2525.** *"well done on SIM;
  keep it up for Drone-2525"* — what he is approving is the method DD-99 used: measure first, report the
  number, name what is NOT measured, and say plainly when the thing he asked about is already correct. That
  method carries to Drone-2525.
- **CONSEQUENCE FOR r.217, which was about to ship under D-41:** the magazine is the likeliest referent of
  *"this"*, so **r.217 does not ship until the revert audit has said whether the thirty-bar fill is work
  that already existed and was lost.** Shipping a rebuild of something that was once already right, without
  first finding out why it stopped being right, would be the class repeating itself a fourth time.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-102, his r0.178 picture names the revert, and it is measured)

> heres one revision where 30 filled up to the top ofnthe magazine

Screenshot persisted as `docs/asks/img/2026.10.11_18.56..53_drone2525_shot92.png` (301,889 B): the corner
reads **eXeL r0.178**, TRAIN UP · LANE 21, `MAG 2 · 30 RDS`, and the gold magazine card is filled with
rounds from just under the feed lips all the way down, the counter reading 30.

### The measurement — his picture and today's deck, profiled identically

Both images were profiled by the same method: find the magazine drawing's ink extent, then find every raster
row where lit pixels cross ≥ 30 % of the body width (that is a round, not a side wall), and express the
first and last such row as a percentage of the drawing's height.

| | fill STARTS | fill ENDS |
|---|---|---|
| **r0.178 — his picture, 30 RDS** | **6.7 % from the top** | 87.2 % |
| **r.215 — shipped today, 30 RDS** | **39.2 % from the top** | 100 % |

Today's deck was rendered headless at 428×926, DPR 3, on the served `play.html`, with
`state.mag = {cap:30, rounds:30}` and `hudMag()` called, and the `#magWell .body` box captured — so it is
the real thing at a real full magazine, not a drawing of one.

**At r0.178 a full magazine read full. Today a full magazine is blank for its top 39 %.** That is the
revert he is pointing at, and his sentence is exactly right.

### What was actually lost — and it is NOT the bar count

The carried `drone-2525_r.178.html` draws the rounds as `#magRibs` with `data-rib="0"` … `data-rib="18"` —
**nineteen ribs, the same count the deck has today.** So the regression is **where the fill sits, not how
many pieces it has**: at r0.178 nineteen ribs spanned essentially the whole magazine body; today nineteen
bars sit in the bottom 61 % of it. The fill lost its top, and the magazine stopped reading as full.

### A correction I owe on my own earlier report

When he reported the magazine a third time I measured r.203 → r.215 and told him the geometry was
**byte-identical across those revisions and that nothing had regressed.** That was true as far as it went
and it was the wrong range. **I never measured back to r0.178**, which is the revision he was naming each
time he said *"this was right a few days ago"*. The regression happened before r.203, in the rework that
turned a drawn, body-spanning fill into a raster cut from a picture — and because every later revision
carried the same raster, a comparison that started at r.203 could only ever report "no change". The
measurement was honest and the window was wrong, which is its own lesson: **when he says a thing used to
be right, the range to measure is back to the revision he names, not back to the last time I touched it.**

### What this does to r.217

- **r.217 is a RESTORATION, not new work**, and the register, the ledger row and the release note must all
  say so. The thirty-bar build already measured **6.5 % → 93.5 %**, which lands on r0.178's 6.7 % → 87.2 %
  at the top and slightly lower at the floor. It restores the behaviour his picture shows.
- **It also does something r0.178 never did**: at thirty bars each round is one bar, so the count is legible
  in the picture rather than only in the number. That half is new and is declared as new.
- **The gate this needs is the one DD-101 is about.** A gate that only compares a revision to the one before
  it can never catch this, because the loss was already in place at r.203. The gate must assert the
  behaviour itself — *at a full magazine the fill reaches the top of the body* — measured on the rendered
  deck, so it is red for every revision from the rework until r.217 and green after.

> We need each R-Core revision slider to work.  The  for each R-Core section (with its own independent
> slider), we need its `</>` at bottom of that section to open up `</>` Core that pulls user to SHOW CODE FOR
> THAT SECTION ONLY.  Good example is Drone-2525
>
> then we show user code for that section (with no user input.  This section should use SIM section as a
> basis (not exactly coded like SIM BEHIND EASTER WGG), which is already coded; as a basis with no ability to
> input code to input own and test.

Three screenshots, persisted as `…_shot93.png` / `…_shot94.png` / `…_shot95.png`:
- **shot93** — the deck at **eXeL r0.215**, TRAIN UP · LANE 21, the R-CORE **icon** alone at the bottom,
  below TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE.
- **shot94** — the same screen one press later: the icon has expanded to the **`R-CORE` wordmark pill**.
- **shot95** — the opened panel, header `R-CORE` / `Version History · Drone-2525 · /main/Dr…`, the revision
  list r8 … r1 with their RELEASE / DECISION / ASK chips and commit citations, the `L5 · constitutional`
  tag. **There is no `</>` anywhere on it, and no slider visible.**

### Reading — DD-104

- **FOUR REQUIREMENTS, and they are per SECTION, not per app.** (1) Every R-CORE section's **revision
  slider works**. (2) Each section has **its own independent slider** — a section's history is its own, which
  is DD-100 again at the level of the control. (3) **Its `</>` sits at the bottom of THAT section** and
  opens `</> Code`. (4) That pane shows **the code for THAT SECTION ONLY** — not the project's whole tree,
  not another section's.
- **"Good example is Drone-2525"** — he is naming the deck as the exemplar, and the deck is the one surface
  measured to have **no chip at all** (`cmpVC` 0, `code-icon` 0, `code-wordmark` 0). His own three
  screenshots walk the two R-CORE beats and end on a panel with nothing at its foot — he pre-empted
  "you did not scroll" for the third time.
- **THE CODE VIEW IS READ-ONLY. This is the sharpening, and it REMOVES scope rather than adding it.**
  *"with no user input"* · *"no ability to input code to input own and test"*. The SIM behind the easter egg
  is the **basis for the FORMAT ONLY** — the numbered section strip, the voxel cube, the plain `<pre>` with
  its `# path` header. What does **not** come across: the **YOUR VERSION editor**, **Check In**, **Submit to
  Simulate**, and the whole challenge/verdict apparatus. *"not exactly coded like SIM BEHIND EASTER EGG"* is
  the instruction that those are left behind.
- **THIS CORRECTS DD-63 AND DD-90 BY SUBTRACTION.** Both said the SIM is the reference format; neither said
  where the resemblance stops. It stops at the editor. A reader of an R-CORE code pane looks; he does not
  type.
- **"EACH R-CORE SECTION" IS THE UNIT THROUGHOUT.** The slider is per section, the `</>` is per section, the
  code shown is per section. Nothing here is global.
- **IT TIES DD-89 TO EVERY SURFACE, not just the deck.** *"We need each R-Core revision slider to work"* —
  the scrubber crash I measured on the deck (`rcRender()` rewrites the whole panel on every `input` tick,
  destroying the slider under his thumb) is stated by him as a requirement across **all** sections. So the
  fix is not a deck patch; it is the class, and each section's panel must be shown to survive a real drag.
- **WHAT IS NOT YET MEASURED, and will be before anything is built:** whether the nine React surfaces'
  sliders also break under a drag, or only the deck's hand-written port; and whether "section" here means a
  PROJECT (Drone-2525, Architect-2525, …) or a section WITHIN a project's code record (the areas the code
  record already declares). His sentence supports the project reading — *"each R-Core section … Good example
  is Drone-2525"* — and that is the reading taken unless he says otherwise.

### DD-105 · Vision-2525 IS THE REFERENCE AND IT IS COMPLETE — read it, never touch it

- **He has now said this twice.** On 2026-10-18: *"ensure you look at Vision-2525 white paper to ensure style
  and memory methods are the same (as Vision-2525 revisions scroll works and Drone-2525 does not)"*. Today:
  *"Slider and revision methods same as Vision-2525 which is already complete."* Under his own law, the
  second telling means the first was not acted on. It had not been — DD-89 was diagnosed from the deck's own
  source and the Vision-2525 implementation was never read.
- **"DO NOT FUCK UP vision-2525"** is an explicit protection order, in capitals. `docs/SOI_VISION2525_LIVING_DOCUMENT.html`
  is a finished, working surface with its own spec (`docs/VISION2525_DOCUMENT_SPEC.md`, locked r40) and its
  own gates. It is the **source to learn from, not a file to edit.** Any revision that touches R-CORE must
  prove Vision-2525's bytes unchanged, and that proof belongs in a gate rather than in my assurance.
- **"use those learnings to make R-CORE FOR EACH PROJECT BETTER"** — the flow is one-way: Vision-2525 →
  every project's R-CORE. Its slider and its "memory methods" (how it holds the reading position across a
  revision change) are the pattern the shared React panel and the deck's vanilla port both adopt.

### DD-106 · EVERY PROJECT'S R-CORE DOWNLOAD MUST BE THE LATEST HTML OF THAT CODE

- *"so others can review how we accomplished Drone-2525 … Or users can see how we accomplished their specific
  innovation project-2525 (SoI-2525, Architect-2525, etc)"* — the download is **for a reader outside this
  session**. Its job is to carry the project's story **and its code** in one file they can open.
- **This is already measured as absent.** The 2026-10-17 audit found that all five downloads sampled carry
  only *Current comparison · What changed · Full record* — **no code at all** — and that two downloads of one
  project **share a filename**, so the second silently overwrites the first. His sentence names both defects
  without having seen that audit.
- "THE LATEST HTML VERSION OF THAT CODE" is read as: the downloaded file is current with the project's newest
  revision at the moment of download, and carries that revision's code, not a stale snapshot.

### DD-107 · THE RESTORE WAS TOO HARD, AND THAT IS THE ARGUMENT FOR SUPABASE (DD-82)

> a magazine restoring to prior version should never be this complicated

- **He is right, and it is worth saying plainly what it actually cost:** a screenshot from him, a pixel
  profile of two images, a five-trail audit across 84 carried revisions and 52 patchers, and a hand-written
  patcher — to put back a fill position that existed at r0.178. **Restoring a prior version should be
  selecting it, not excavating it.**
- **This gives DD-82 its reason.** Until now "all R-CORE history in Supabase SQL" was recorded as a
  durability measure. His sentence reframes it: the point is **restore**. A code history that can be queried
  and restored makes "put the magazine back the way it was at r0.178" a selection, and makes the revert class
  (DD-101) structurally harder to fall into.
- **One thing I must not overstate, and it is already on the record:** Supabase as it stands today is a
  single free-tier project with no replica that has already paused once, and the keys are not set in the
  build. So the honest shape is **git kept as the second store and Supabase as the queryable, restorable
  first** — not a move off GitHub. Said now rather than discovered later.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-108, "Stop removing The Range at top of QR" — and his phone is seven revisions behind)

> Stop removing The Range at top of QR

Screenshot persisted as `…_shot96.png`: the QR sheet reading **`LANE 21 · TURRET · TRAIN UP`** in green caps
with **no map name above it**, the link `…/play.html?range=21&role=turret&mode=train-up`, COPY LINK beneath.
**The corner reads `eXeL r0.208`.**

### Reading — DD-108, and the measurement comes before the apology

- **MEASURED FIRST, because "stop removing" presumes it was removed.** In the served deck today,
  `play.html` composes the sheet as **`title.textContent = rungTitle()` → "The Range"**, with
  `Lane 21 · Turret · Mode: Train - Up` beneath it and `Scan to share The Range` as the hint. **The map name
  is there and has been since r.209.** So the heading is not removed from the code.
- **HE IS LOOKING AT r0.208. The live deck is r0.215.** His picture is **seven revisions stale**, and at
  r.208 the one-line caps heading without the map name is exactly what that revision drew. r.209 added
  `THE RANGE · LANE 21 · …`; r.211 made it his picture's two-line title case.
- **THAT IS NOT HIS FAULT AND IT IS NOT "JUST CACHE" — IT IS A DELIVERY DEFECT I HAVE NOT PROVEN FIXED.**
  The cache class (S1) shipped `no-cache, must-revalidate` for HTML documents precisely so a stale tab could
  not happen, and **I never confirmed it took effect.** The one check that would settle it —
  `curl -sSI <url>/drone-2525/play.html | grep -i cache-control` — was handed to him and never run, so the
  fix is **UNVERIFIED to this day**. A second phone screenshot at r0.208 (he was also at r0.208 when he
  reported the magazine) says the same tab has been stuck for a while.
- **AND `rungWord()` IS DEAD CODE, which is worth recording even though it is not the defect.** It is
  defined and referenced nowhere but its own comment; r.211 introduced `rungTitle()` beside it for his
  picture's title case and left `rungWord()` standing because the boot row and `drone-playable` pin it. A
  pinned function with no caller is a gate guarding nothing — named here, not fixed in r.216.
- **WHAT THIS ACTUALLY OWES HIM, in order:** (1) prove the cache header is live, with the response header
  quoted, not asserted; (2) if it is live, find why his tab still served r0.208; (3) only then look at the
  heading, which measurement says is already correct. **Fixing the heading first would be fixing a screen
  that is not broken while the thing that stopped him seeing seven revisions of work goes unexamined.**

### Grok's first report — what it confirms, and the one thing it found that I had not

**CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY, and it matches my own measurement exactly:** the photo is **r0.208**, the served
deck is **r0.215**, the sheet title is already `The Range` from `rungTitle()`, the hint already reads
*"Scan to share The Range"*, and the deep link in the photo is correct. Its advice — *"Do not patch the
sheet against this photo … A hard refresh until the corner reads r0.215, then open the QR again, is the
test"* — is the same conclusion DD-108 reached from the bytes.

**NEW, AND I VERIFIED IT MYSELF RATHER THAN TAKE IT ON TRUST (DD-110):**

> #qrUrl is word-break: break-all. The photo splits the host as drone / -2525. That rule is still in the
> served CSS.

Measured in **both** the served `play.html` and the r.216 cut:

```
#qrUrl{color:#8E9BA3;font-size:13px;line-height:1.45;text-align:center;max-width:22em;margin:0 0 22px;word-break:break-all}
```

`break-all` breaks greedily at **every** character once a line is full, so it splits the host name itself —
`…/drone` / `-2525/play.html…` — which is what his photo shows and which will still happen at r0.215. It is
**not** a stale-tab artefact. A URL should break at its own seams. **Recorded as DD-110 and NOT folded into
r.216**, which is the magazine and ships as a clean restoration; it is its own small revision.

**Its other observations, each checked against the record rather than accepted:** the magazine row in the
photo is the approved picture at r0.208 (true — the row is r.202/r.203's and unchanged since); the green
lamp reading FIRE while the strip says PRESS TARGET is **r0.208 behaviour that r.204–r.206 already fixed**,
so its own instruction to "recheck that only on r0.215" is right; and the zoom law (Train Up 30×, Qual 3×)
is as written, not a defect.

### Reading — DD-113

- **IT TESTED r0.216, AND THE RANGE PASSES 42/42 ON ALL THREE DRILLS.** That is the first independent
  confirmation that the Range is whole across every lane, not just lane 21.
- **FIVE FINDINGS ARE NEW TO THIS RECORD and none of them were in my own measurements:**
  1. **The ◀ T / T ▶ station walkers EXIST and WORK, but their panel measures 0×0** — so the capability is
     there and is simply unreachable by a finger. That changes the cost of DD-111 item 3 from "build a
     station control" to "surface one that already works".
  2. **A 43rd body, `T1`, labelled TURRET, parked on the range at lane 21**, sorting between turret 9 and
     turret 10 **because the ids sort as text** (`T1` < `T10`). A data defect with a visible symptom.
  3. **The craft select offers `T1`, not `T01`–`T42`**, so on a lawn seat it draws an empty box.
  4. **The magazine is not reloaded on entry to the lawn** — it arrived with 10 rounds left.
  5. **The station ring geometry, measured:** T01 (0, 240), T22 (0, −240), the rest evenly between.
- **IT ALSO CONTRADICTS ITS OWN EARLIER REPORT, and the contradiction is the useful part.** Its first pass
  said the status line *"must not stay on PRESS TARGET"*. This pass reports Train Up working correctly —
  amber, red, the plate falls. But it reports **Qual 40** showing `50 M RIGHT · PRESS TARGET` while a plate
  stood. **So the status-line defect, if it exists on r.216, is a QUAL-40 case and not the general one** —
  which is exactly why nothing was changed there on the first report. That single case is now the thing to
  measure.
- **ITS FIX ORDER MATCHES DD-111 AND SHARPENS IT**, and item 5 is already DD-110/DD-111's link work.
- **"A shot taken after that switch scored a range plate, not a lawn plate"** is the sharpest sentence in the
  report: the teleport is not merely navigational, it silently moves the player's rounds onto another map's
  targets. That belongs in the fire gate's own invariant.
