  gates are now true on the live glass as well as in CI.**
- **THE STATUS STRIP IS CORRECT AND THE FIRST REPORT WAS MISREAD — including by its own author.** The
  strip walks `PRESS TARGET → PRESS APPROVE → PRESS FIRE → TARGET FIRST` in all three drills, **after a hit
  is already on the board**, which is precisely the case r.204 was built for. **Refusing to "fix" it on the
  first report was right**, and the reason is now on the record: the sentence being complained about was
  never the strip.
- **THE REAL DEFECT IS NARROW AND IS THE EXACT INVERSE OF HIS OWN LAW.** DD-111 said *"The toast must not
  be the only place that tells the truth."* The measured defect is that **the toast OUTLIVES the truth**:
  after FIRE it reads `HIT · 50 M RIGHT · TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE` and **stays up** while the strip has
  already moved on to `TARGET FIRST`. A player reading the screen sees two statements, one current and one
  stale, and the stale one is the louder.
- **IT IS DRILL-DEPENDENT, which is the clue to the fix:** Train Up and Qual 40 fire that toast; **Train
  Down does not** — it is skipped when the drill is `stay`. So the toast's lifetime, not the strip's logic,
  is the thing to change, and one drill already demonstrates the wanted behaviour.
- **THIS IS A SMALL, CLEAN REVISION** and it pairs naturally with DD-110 (`word-break:break-all` splitting
  the host in the share sheet): both are *the glass saying something it should not*. Proposed as one
  revision — **nothing on the glass outlives the truth** — rather than two.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-115, r.217 approved, and the lawn's targets go where air-defence assets would go)

> fix to release 217; and then work through ensuring targets for each turret on capital lawn are accessibleX
> to turrets are in strategic locations like air defense assets would be, and work through turrets before
> quad quadcopter mode for Train Up, Train Down, and QUAL-40  for single player first before preparing:
>
> any dual player on an Quad (Drone Operator + Turret Operator).

### Reading — DD-115

- **r.217 IS APPROVED AS PROPOSED** — the post-FIRE toast must not outlive the truth, and the share sheet's
  address must not break mid-word. One revision, one invariant: *nothing on the glass outlives the truth,
  and nothing on it is broken mid-word.*
- **THE LAWN'S TARGETS ARE NOT A FIRING-RANGE LADDER ANY MORE.** His earlier answer was *"a range laid out
  on the lawn — silhouettes in front of the station at 50/100/150/200/250/300 m, left, centre and right"*.
  Today he says **"strategic locations like air defense assets would be"**. Those are different pictures: a
  ladder is a qualification lane; air-defence siting is emplacements chosen to cover approaches and protect
  what matters. **The newer sentence governs.**
- **ONE GENUINE AMBIGUITY, AND IT CHANGES THE BUILD, so it is put to him rather than guessed.** His sentence
  can be read two ways: (a) the **TARGETS** stand where air-defence assets would stand — so the lawn is
  dotted with emplacement-like positions a turret engages; or (b) the **TURRETS** are the air-defence assets
  and it is their 42 stations that must be strategically sited, with targets then placed to be engageable
  from them. Reading (a) places targets; reading (b) re-places the ring. **Not guessed. Asked.**
- **"accessibleX to turrets"** is the acceptance test and it is already measured as failing: today the sight
  locks nothing from any station, because `lockOn` caps pop-ups at 80 m while every station stands 240 m
  out. Whatever the siting, **every target must be lockable from the station that owns it** — that is the
  gate.
- **THE ORDER IS EXPLICIT AND NARROWS EARLIER SCOPE:** turrets before the quad; all three drills; **single
  player first**; and only then *"preparing"* the dual-player Quad (Drone Operator + Turret Operator). The
  word "preparing" matters — the 1v1 four-player rung from DD-109 is to be made ready, not built, in this
  pass.

## Addendum (operator decisions, 2026.10.19 — D-45 · D-46, the lawn's siting and its targets)

| # | asked | his answer |
|---|---|---|
| **D-45** | which is sited like air defence — the targets or the turrets? | **The TURRETS are the air-defence assets** |
| **D-46** | what is a lawn target? | **Wireframe air-defence pieces** |

### Readings

- **D-45 — THE 42 STATIONS MOVE.** They come off the even 240 m ring (T01 at (0, 240), T22 at (0, −240),
  the rest evenly between — Grok's measurement) and go where air defence would actually be emplaced:
  covering the approaches, with overlapping arcs, protecting the dome. **Targets are then placed to be
  engageable from those new positions**, which is the order of operations — site the seats first, then the
  threat picture they answer.
  **This supersedes the even ring**, and it is the larger of the two options he was offered; he took it
  deliberately. It also means **Grok's station map is about to change**, and his tester should be told
  rather than left to re-derive it.
- **D-46 — THE TARGETS ARE WIREFRAME AIR-DEFENCE PIECES**: launchers, radars, vehicles, drawn in the 1983
  wireframe as the Capitol is, under the vector law (edges only, the 13 colours, no fill). **Not the
  Range's silhouettes.** Each piece needs its own drawing and its own hit geometry, so this is a build, not
  a placement — again the larger option, taken deliberately.
- **TOGETHER THEY DESCRIBE A COHERENT SCENE, and it is worth stating so the intent is not lost:** our 42
  turrets are friendly air defence, sited as air defence is sited; what they engage is enemy air-defence
  materiel. That is a counter-air-defence drill on the Capitol lawn, not a qualification lane with a dome
  behind it — which is exactly the distinction Grok drew.
- **WHAT THIS DOES TO THE EARLIER ANSWER:** his previous lawn answer (silhouettes at 50/100/150/200/250/300
  m, left/centre/right in front of each station) is **superseded in both halves** — the ladder is gone and
  the silhouettes are gone. Recorded as superseded, not deleted; the earlier answer stays on the record
  where it was given.
- **THE ACCEPTANCE TEST IS UNCHANGED AND IS STILL THE HARD PART:** every target must be **lockable from the
  station that owns it**. Today `lockOn` caps pop-ups at 80 m and every station stands 240 m out, so
  whatever the new siting, the reach rule is the gate the lawn lives or dies on.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-116, Grok's seven pass lines for r.217, and the correction of my correction)

> Claude's pairing is right. Both faults are the glass saying something it should not. The strip is not one
> of them.
>
> **What r.216 actually does** — the hit toast is `HIT · <plate> · TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE`. Raised on Train
> Up and Qual 40, skipped when the drill is stay. Its timer is 45 × length, clamped 1.4 s to 4.2 s; the
> sentence is 41 characters, so it stays up about 1.8 s.
>
> **I watched it outlive the strip.** After the 50 m left plate fell:
>
> | Moment | Strip | Toast |
> |---|---|---|
> | Just after FIRE | TARGET FIRST | HIT · 50 M LEFT · TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE |
> | Sight already on 50 m right | 50 M RIGHT · PRESS TARGET | same sentence, still up |
>
> Two statements, one current and one stale. The stale one is the louder. Train Down does not do this.
> The share line is still `#qrSheet #qrUrl { word-break: break-all }`.
>
> **Pass lines for r.217**
> 1. Train Up, after a hit is already on the board. FIRE the next plate. The toast must not read
>    TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE. If it names the hit, it names that plate and that it is down, and **it is gone
>    once the strip names a different step.** While the strip says 50 M RIGHT · PRESS TARGET, the toast must
>    not still be talking about 50 m left.
> 2. Qual 40. Same rule. One plate up is enough.
> 3. Train Down. **Still no recital toast. Do not add one to make the drills match.**
> 4. The strip does not move. After a scored hit it still walks PRESS TARGET → PRESS APPROVE → PRESS FIRE →
>    TARGET FIRST in all three drills.
> 5. The magazine does not move. Thirty-one different clips, 30 at 6.5% and 20 at 36.7%, green 30–20, amber
>    19–6, red 5–0.
> 6. Share sheet. The address is not broken inside a word. explore-096 and workers.dev each stay whole.
> 7. The lawn stays held. No station list, ?map= and ?station= still unread, reach still 80 m against a
>    240 m ring.
>
> Say when r.217 is the deck on the glass and I will run those seven.

### Reading — DD-116, and I have to correct my own correction

- **I OVER-CORRECTED, AND GROK'S OBSERVATION IS THE RIGHT ONE.** I drove the deck, measured the toast
  hiding at ~1.9 s while the strip sat on `TARGET FIRST` until t+4000 ms, and told him the toast *"does not
  outlive the truth"*. **My probe never moved the sight.** Grok's did — and within that 1.8 s window the
  strip advances to the NEXT plate while the toast is still naming the LAST one. So the toast does not
  persist for ever, but **it does outlive the thing it is about**, which is the defect that matters. My
  first framing was right, my correction of it was wrong, and the difference between the two probes is one
  action a player takes constantly.
- **THE FIX IS THEREFORE A DISMISSAL, NOT A TIMEOUT.** The hit toast is cleared **when the strip names a
  different step or a different plate** — exactly Grok's pass line 1. Changing the clamp would not fix it;
  a faster toast would still be stale for whatever time it had.
- **AND THE RECITAL GOES.** `TARGET · APPROVE · FIRE` reads as an instruction to do three things when all
  three have just been done. If the toast names the hit it names *that plate and that it is down*.
- **PASS LINE 3 IS A GUARD AGAINST ME, and it is a good one.** *"Train Down. Still no recital toast. Do not
  add one to make the drills match."* The tidy instinct is to make the three drills symmetrical; he is
  telling me not to. Train Down's silence is correct and stays.
- **PASS LINES 4 AND 5 ARE NO-REGRESSION LINES** on the strip and on r.216's magazine — the two things most
  likely to be broken by a change in this area, named in advance. They become gate legs, not just checks.
- **THESE SEVEN ARE THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA FOR r.217** and are written into the revision's notes as such.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-117, the air-defence placement lesson, and it contradicts D-46)

Relayed from Grok, verbatim in substance:

> FM 30 is the old intelligence series. The air-defense manual is **FM 3-01, Air and Missile Defense
> Operations, 26 August 2025**. Avenger employment is **ATP 3-01.64** (before that, FM 44-44). Sentinel is
> **FM 3-01.48**. THAAD sits in the same FM 3-01 as the upper tier.
>
> **The sentence that settles Claude's question: the guns and the radars ARE the defense. The target is the
> air track coming at the thing they are defending.**
>
> **Six principles** (FM 3-01): mass, mix, mobility, integration, flexibility, agility.
> **Seven siting tenets:** mutual support (one weapon fires into the dead ground of the next) · overlapping
> fires (envelopes cross in range and height) · overlapping coverage (sensor pictures cross, no seam) ·
> balanced fires (equal volume all round, avenue unknown) · weighted coverage (most volume on the likely
> avenue) · early engagement (hit before release) · defense in depth (under fire the whole way in) ·
> resilience (still works after a loss). **Weighted and balanced are a trade.** Early engagement costs
> balance too.
> **Sensors go in first**, on ground with the longest line of sight, so the picture has no gap; **weapons
> second**, so the defended asset sits inside their fire (FM 3-01, 4-73).
>
> **THAAD** — upper tier, ~90 soldiers, six launchers × eight interceptors, one AN/TPY-2, a fire-control
> node. Defense drawn from the **launcher footprint over the asset**, not from the radar; the radar's
> primary target line points at the threat axis. Known axis → weighted; tracks from anywhere → balanced.
> Planned first, Patriot and SHORAD under it. **"It does not belong on a 240 m ring around a building."**
> **Sentinel (AN/MPQ-64)** — does not shoot. X-band 360° search, cue and track for the guns. Teams in pairs
> so pictures overlap; a site needs line of sight, a primary and an alternate, two ways in and out, and a
> weapon close enough to protect it. **The 2003 "no farther than 60 km, 20 km overlap" is the OLD, shorter
> radar — do not teach that number as current.**
> **Avenger** — the gun the player sits in. Light vehicle, day/night, low altitude, against UAS,
> helicopters, low fixed-wing, low missiles. Fights from flank, rear or overwatch, **on the air avenue**,
> not inside a ground fight. Needs room to spread, two ways in and out, and a place the next gun can cover.
> Six fire units = two sections of three.
>
> **On the Capitol lawn — the Capitol is the defended asset. It is not a target.**
> • One THAAD-type site **off** the lawn, oriented on a chosen threat axis, footprint over the building.
>   Not one of 42 identical stations.
> • Sentinel pairs on ground that can see, behind the guns, pictures overlapping, each with a primary and
>   an alternate. **They cue. They are not shot, and they do not shoot.**
> • Avengers on the avenues in: **Congress, the Great Walk, the allees.** Spaced so one covers the next
>   gun's dead ground. Weighted toward the avenue you name. **A ring all the way around is the
>   balanced-fires case, and only when the track really can come from any direction — the small-UAS case.**
>
> **What pops up, and what the player may target, approve, and fire, is the AIR TRACK on that avenue. Not
> the turret. Not the radar. Not a person.**
>
> The Range stays the rehearsal of the three presses. The lawn is the same three presses on a defense that
> was sited this way. An incoming-track lesson can be persisted as its own ask. It does not replace the
> pop-ups, and it does not make the stations into targets.
> **r.217 is still only the toast and the share line. This placement is the lesson to write down before
> anyone moves a station.**

### Reading — DD-117

- **IT CONFIRMS D-45 AND CONTRADICTS D-46, and the contradiction must be put to him rather than resolved by
  me.** D-45 (the TURRETS are the air-defence assets) is exactly what this says. **D-46 said the targets are
  "wireframe air-defence pieces — launchers, radars, vehicles."** This briefing says those pieces ARE the
  defence, on our side, and that what the player engages is **the air track**. Both cannot be built.
- **IT ALSO CONTRADICTS THE EVEN 240 m RING IN A SPECIFIC WAY**, which is useful: a full ring is not wrong
  in general — it is the **balanced-fires** case, correct only when the threat can come from any direction,
  i.e. small UAS. Weighted coverage on a named avenue is the alternative, and the two are a declared trade.
  So "re-site the 42 stations" (D-45) has a doctrinal shape: **name the avenue, or declare the ring as the
  balanced case on purpose.**
- **THE ORDER OF SITING IS A BUILD ORDER TOO:** sensors first on the longest line of sight, weapons second
  so the asset sits inside their fire. If the lawn ever grows sensors, they are placed before the guns.
- **GROK HAS BOUNDED ITS OWN BRIEFING, and that bound is respected:** *"r.217 is still only the toast and
  the share line. This placement is the lesson to write down before anyone moves a station."* Nothing in
  DD-117 enters r.217, and no station moves until the D-46 conflict is settled.
- **THE SOURCE CORRECTION IS KEPT:** FM 3-01 (26 Aug 2025), ATP 3-01.64 for Avenger, FM 3-01.48 for
  Sentinel — not the FM 30 intelligence series, and not FM 44-44's superseded numbers.

## Addendum (operator decisions, 2026.10.19 — D-47 · D-48, the targets are air AND ground, and the siting is mine to decide)

| # | asked | his answer, verbatim |
|---|---|---|
| **D-47** | air tracks (doctrine) or ground air-defence pieces (D-46)? | **"targets are Air and Ground threats"** |
| **D-48** | how are the 42 sited if they come off the ring? | **"decide placement of 42 new turrets"** |

### Readings

- **D-47 SUPERSEDES D-46 AND GOES WIDER THAN EITHER OPTION OFFERED.** Neither "air tracks only" nor "ground
  pieces only": **both**. So the lawn's pop-ups are a mixed threat set — air tracks on the avenues (UAS,
  helicopter, low fixed-wing) **and** ground threats — and the same TARGET → APPROVE → FIRE answers both.
  That is consistent with FM 3-01 on the air side and keeps D-46's ground materiel as a second kind of
  contact rather than discarding it.
  **One thing this does NOT change, and it is the sentence that caused the conflict:** our 42 turrets and
  any sensors remain **the defence**. They are never the target. A ground threat is something attacking the
  defended asset, not one of our own emplacements.
- **D-48 DELEGATES THE SITING TO ME, EXPLICITLY, and says the 42 are NEW.** *"decide placement of 42 new
  turrets"* — so the even 240 m ring is retired and the replacement positions are my decision under his
  standing sign-off *"approve all your own decisions; document changes"*. He is not going to name the
  avenue; I am.
  **What that obliges, and it is written here so the decision can be judged rather than taken on trust:**
  the placement is designed to FM 3-01's tenets — mutual support (each gun covers the next one's dead
  ground), overlapping fires and coverage, **weighted** toward the named avenues (Congress, the Great Walk,
  the allees) with the weighting stated, defense in depth so a track is engaged the whole way in, and
  resilience so the defence still works with guns lost. Sensors, if any are ever added, go in **before**
  the guns. Every position, its arc, and the reason for it is recorded, and the acceptance test stands:
  **every target must be lockable from the station that owns it.**
- **NOTHING MOVES YET.** Grok's bound is unchallenged by either answer: *"r.217 is still only the toast and
  the share line. This placement is the lesson to write down before anyone moves a station."* The siting is
  designed and recorded first, then built.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-118, the siting is settled: D-46 withdrawn, D-47 narrowed, D-48 is NOT 42)

Relayed from Grok, verbatim:

> Claude is right about the order and wrong about the count. Settle it like this.
> **r.217 ships first. Toast and share line only. Nothing on the lawn moves. The seven pass lines stand.**
> I test them when that deck is on the glass.
> **D-45 stands.** The guns and the radars are the defense. They are never the target.
> **D-46 is withdrawn.** Do not build wireframe air-defense pieces as targets.
> **D-47 does not add ground.** This replacement engages the air track only. A ground contact is a later
> ask. It is not a second kind of target in this build.
> **D-48 is not 42 new turrets. Forty-two was the even ring. Designing 42 new ones recreates it.** The
> tenets Claude wrote are the right test, and **the mix** is the thing that has to pass them:
> • Avengers on Congress, the Great Walk, and the allees. Each gun covers the next gun's dead ground. The
>   weighting is stated.
> • Sentinel pairs behind them. Pictures overlap. **They cue. They are not seats.**
> • One THAAD sector off the lawn, primary target line on the named axis, footprint over the Capitol.
> • A track is under fire the whole way in. Losing a gun does not open a seam.
> • Every air track is lockable from the station that owns it.
> **The 240 m ring is not deleted. It is the balanced-fires case**, correct only when the track can come
> from any direction — the small-UAS case. It stays on the record as a named option. **The default that
> gets designed first is the weighted avenues, not a new circle of 42.**
> **Record every position, its arc, and its reason before a station moves.** Then build the lawn to that
> record. Not before.

### Reading — DD-118

- **THREE OF HIS OWN ANSWERS ARE AMENDED BY THIS RELAY, and the trail is kept honest about that.** D-46
  ("wireframe air-defence pieces" as targets) is **withdrawn**. D-47 ("targets are Air and Ground threats")
  is **narrowed to air only for this build**, with ground kept as a later ask rather than discarded. D-48
  ("decide placement of 42 new turrets") is **corrected on the count**. He relayed the correction himself,
  so it governs — but D-46, D-47 and D-48 were his own words and are recorded as amended, not as though
  they were never given.
- **THE COUNT WAS THE ERROR AND IT WAS MINE TO CATCH.** *"Forty-two was the even ring. Designing 42 new
  ones recreates it."* I had taken "42 new turrets" literally and briefed a 42-position design — which
  would have reproduced the thing being replaced with different coordinates. **The number is an output of
  the siting, not an input to it.** The mix is designed to the tenets; however many guns that takes is how
  many there are.
- **THE MIX IS NOW SPECIFIED, and it is three kinds of thing, not one:** Avenger fire units on the named
  avenues (Congress, the Great Walk, the allees), **Sentinel pairs behind them that cue and are NOT seats**,
  and **one THAAD sector off the lawn** with its primary target line on the named axis and its footprint
  over the Capitol. Only the Avengers are player seats.
- **THE RING IS PRESERVED AS A NAMED OPTION, not deleted** — the balanced-fires case for small UAS, correct
  when the track can come from any direction. That is a better outcome than retiring it, because it keeps
  the doctrinal trade visible instead of replacing one default with another.
- **THE ACCEPTANCE TESTS ARE UNCHANGED** and now number five: dead-ground coverage between adjacent guns,
  stated weighting, depth (under fire the whole way in), resilience (a lost gun opens no seam), and every
  air track lockable from the station that owns it.
- **THE ORDER IS EXPLICIT AND BINDING:** r.217 first, toast and share line only, nothing on the lawn moves;
  then the record of every position, arc and reason; **then** the build. Not before.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-119, the air-only bound is withdrawn: three threat classes, one acceptance line)

Relayed from Grok, verbatim:

> **The air-only bound is withdrawn. The test covers both. The defense is still not a target.**
> **Air.** A hostile air track, in the hostile air frame. It pops on an avenue. The Avenger that owns that
> avenue can lock it. Target, Approve, Fire drops it. Sentinel cues it. **Sentinel is not the seat.**
> **Ground.** A hostile ground threat, in the hostile ground frame, coming at the Capitol. **It is not an
> Avenger, not a Sentinel, not a THAAD, and not a person.** The station that owns that avenue can lock it,
> and the same three presses drop it.
> **Incoming rounds stay with C-RAM.** Rockets, artillery, and mortars are the LPWS test, not the ground
> test. A round in the LPWS arc can be locked from that gun. **The gun itself stays friendly.**
> **One acceptance line for the lawn, when it is built: every air track, every ground threat, and every
> incoming round is lockable from the station that owns it, and no friendly icon ever takes a hit.**
> **r.217 does not grow to include this.** The toast and the share line ship first.

### Reading — DD-119

- **D-47 IS RESTORED AND EXTENDED.** DD-118 narrowed the build to air only; that narrowing is now withdrawn
  by the same source that imposed it. The lawn carries **three threat classes**, not one and not two:
  **air track · ground threat · incoming round (RAM)**. His original D-47 answer — "Air and Ground" — was
  right, and the third class is new.
- **THE THIRD CLASS IS THE ADDITION, and it is properly separated:** rockets, artillery and mortars are
  **not** ground threats — they are the **C-RAM / LPWS** test, locked from that gun within its arc.
  Conflating RAM with ground would have put a mortar round in the wrong frame and the wrong gun.
- **EVERY PIECE IS ALREADY IN eXeL-STD-2525, so none of it is invented:** `C-RAM / LPWS` is in the asset
  catalogue with its wireframe cue (20 mm six-barrel Gatling under a white dome radome, trailer-mounted)
  and its SIDC `S*G*EWA---` (AD gun: dome over vertical gun line), role "Point defense vs RAM + UAS". The
  hostile frames — top-half diamond open bottom for air, diamond for ground — are in the affiliation frame
  law. **The catalogue anticipated this before the ask.**
- **ONE ACCEPTANCE LINE NOW COVERS THE WHOLE LAWN**, and it has two halves, the second of which is new and
  is a safety property rather than a capability: *every air track, every ground threat and every incoming
  round is lockable from the station that owns it*, **and no friendly icon ever takes a hit.** The second
  half is the mechanical form of "the defense is never the target" and belongs in the fire gate.
- **FOUR SEAT TYPES ARE NOW IMPLIED, and only some are seats:** Avenger (seat, air + ground on its avenue),
  **C-RAM / LPWS (seat, RAM within its arc)**, Sentinel (**cues, not a seat**), THAAD (sector off the lawn,
  not a seat). That is a change from DD-118's single seat type and it must be reflected in the siting.
- **r.217 IS BOUNDED AGAIN, for the third time and by the same source:** toast and share line only.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-120, the lawn's shooting modes are higher complexity than the Range's)

> for capital lawn, we have higher complexity shooting modes for turrets

### Reading — DD-120

- **THIS CHANGES AN ASSUMPTION THAT HAS BEEN CARRIED SINCE DD-111 WITHOUT BEING QUESTIONED.** Grok's
  specification said *"Train Up, Train Down, and Qual 40 run on the lawn plates"* — i.e. the Range's three
  drills, ported unchanged onto a new map. His sentence says the lawn's modes are **higher complexity**,
  which is not the same thing as the same drills over different ground.
- **IT IS CONSISTENT WITH EVERYTHING DECIDED IN THE LAST HOUR, and that is why it reads as deliberate
  rather than casual.** The lawn now has **three threat classes** (air track, ground threat, incoming
  round), **two seat types** (Avenger, C-RAM/LPWS), a cueing sensor that is not a seat, a sector weapon
  off the map, named avenues with stated weighting, and a resilience requirement. **A drill designed for
  eleven static plates in one lane cannot exercise any of that.** The Range's three were built for a
  qualification lane; the lawn is a defence.
- **WHAT "HIGHER COMPLEXITY" MIGHT MEAN IS GENUINELY OPEN, and I am not guessing it.** Three readings each
  build something different: (a) the same three drills but against **moving** threats that fly or close on
  an avenue, rather than plates that stand and fall; (b) **new modes** beyond the three — a defence
  scenario held for a duration against mixed waves; (c) **engagement discipline** — several threats up at
  once, across classes, where the player must engage in the right order and a wrong priority costs.
  They are not exclusive, and two of them could be one mode.
- **AND A SECOND THING IS OPEN:** whether the Range's three drills still appear on the lawn at all, or
  whether the lawn has its own set. Grok has already said *"The Range stays the rehearsal of the three
  presses"*, which argues the Range keeps its three unchanged — but it does not say what the lawn offers
  in their place.
- **BOTH QUESTIONS ARE PUT TO HIM.** This is the third design fork in an hour where guessing would have
  cost a rebuild, and the last two guesses — 42 positions, and air-only — were both wrong.
- **r.217 IS UNAFFECTED.** Toast and share line only; nothing here enters it.

## Addendum (operator decisions, 2026.10.19 — D-49 · D-50, what the lawn's complexity is, and the three still run there)

> **D-49** — what makes the lawn's modes higher complexity? → **"Several up at once, across classes"**
> **D-50** — do Train Up, Train Down and Qual 40 still appear on the lawn? → **"The three, plus the harder ones"**

*(Numbering note: these are the D- decision series. D-50 here is not DD-50, the SC II match-setup window.)*

### Readings

- **D-49 — THE COMPLEXITY IS SIMULTANEITY AND PRIORITY, AND NOTHING ELSE.** Several threats live at once,
  **across the three classes** — air track, ground threat, incoming round — and the player chooses what to
  engage first. A wrong order costs: a leaker gets through while the easy one is being shot.
- **WHAT HE DID NOT CHOOSE IS AS IMPORTANT, because the question allowed several and he took one.** He did
  **not** choose moving threats, did **not** choose a hold-the-Capitol scored scenario, and did **not**
  choose depth-and-handover between stations. **So the lawn's contacts stay POP-UPS, as his original brief
  said ("test pop ups for 3 modes"), and they do not fly or close.** That is far cheaper than moving tracks
  and it keeps the whole existing pop machinery. Those three are **not in scope** and are recorded as
  offered-and-declined, not as forgotten.
- **ONE TENSION, STATED NOT SMOOTHED:** an "air track" that does not move is a static contact in an air
  frame. That is internally consistent with a pop-up drill and with his brief, but it is worth his knowing
  it is what was chosen, because "track" normally implies motion. **One word reinstates movement later; it
  is simply not this build.**
- **D-50 — THE LAWN CARRIES FIVE-ISH MODES, NOT THREE AND NOT A SEPARATE SET.** Train Up, Train Down and
  Qual 40 run on the lawn **as a warm-up**, with the higher-complexity mode(s) **alongside** them. So the
  three drills are ported AND the new one is built. The Range is unchanged and keeps its own three as the
  rehearsal of the three presses.
- **THE SCOPE THIS SETS, plainly:** the lawn needs the three ported drills, plus at least one mode in which
  **multiple contacts of different classes are up simultaneously** and engagement order matters. The
  acceptance line from DD-119 is unchanged and now has to hold with several contacts live: *every air
  track, every ground threat and every incoming round is lockable from the station that owns it, and no
  friendly icon ever takes a hit.*
- **r.217 IS UNAFFECTED** — toast and share line only.

## Addendum (operator, 2026.10.19 — DD-121, the lawn's eight fire modes, the seat-to-mode map, and two failure conditions)

Relayed from Grok, verbatim:

> The Range keeps the simple selector. **The Capitol turrets do not use it.** A turret mode decides whether
> this seat may fire, how many leave, and whether a second one is already committed. **Target, Approve,
> Fire stays in front of all of them.**
>
> | Mode | What one approval allows | Refused when |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Hold** | Nothing | The track is outside the arc, or it is not this seat's class |
> | **Cue** | The optic slews to the named track. No round leaves until Fire | No sensor has named it |
> | **Single** | One round or one interceptor | The magazine or the rail is empty |
> | **Shoot-look-shoot** | One round. The result shows. A second round needs a new Approve | The track is already down |
> | **Ripple** | Two rounds, one Approve, the second already committed | Fewer than two are left |
> | **Salvo** | Two interceptors together, one Approve | This is not a Patriot or a THAAD seat |
> | **Sector** | Fire only inside the arc written for that gun | The track is in the sight but outside the arc |
> | **Layer** | The seat takes only its own class | An Avenger is offered a mortar, or an LPWS is offered a high track |
>
> **Which seat wears which mode:**
> • An **Avenger** on an avenue takes **air**. Single, shoot-look-shoot, and ripple. It does not take a
>   mortar, and it does not take a ground threat **unless that seat was recorded for ground**.
> • An **LPWS** on the Capitol takes the **incoming round**. Its burst is the **C-RAM burst, not three rifle
>   rounds.** It does not take an air track that belongs to an Avenger.
> • A **ground seat**, if one is recorded, takes the **ground threat only**. Same three presses.
> • **THAAD and Patriot** take the **high track**. Single, shoot-look-shoot, or salvo. **Not the 30-round
>   picture.**
> • **Sentinel never wears a fire mode. It cues.**
>
> The strip names the mode and the reason for a refusal, **in one line**: `HOLD · NOT IN ARC`,
> `HOLD · OTHER SEAT`, `RIPPLE · NEED 2`. **It does not recite all three presses after the shot.**
>
> **Every round that leaves still changes the picture, one step per round. A ripple that moves the picture
> once has failed. A friendly icon that takes a hit has failed.**
>
> This is not r.217. The toast and the share line still ship alone. These modes are written into the lawn
> record with each gun's arc, and **they are tested only after that record is what the lawn was built
> from.**

### Reading — DD-121

- **THE RANGE AND THE LAWN NOW HAVE DIFFERENT CONTROLS, AND THAT IS THE HEADLINE.** *"The Range keeps the
  simple selector. The Capitol turrets do not use it."* So `#rngMode`'s three-drill select is Range-only;
  the lawn's seats carry a **fire mode** that answers three questions — may this seat fire, how many rounds
  leave, and is a second already committed.
- **THE THREE PRESSES SURVIVE UNCHANGED AND THAT IS SAID EXPLICITLY.** *"Target, Approve, Fire stays in
  front of all of them."* **What a mode changes is how many rounds ONE approval releases** — and because
  the mode is chosen before the approval, Ripple's second round is *"already committed"* rather than
  unapproved. That reconciles Ripple with the standing law that a shot with no approval does not score.
- **r.216 AND r.217 BOTH EXTEND INTO THIS CLEANLY, which is a good sign the chain is coherent:**
  *"Every round that leaves still changes the picture, one step per round"* is r.216's magazine gate carried
  forward — **and it sharpens into a new failure: a ripple that moves the picture ONCE has failed**, because
  two rounds left. And *"It does not recite all three presses after the shot"* is exactly the toast defect
  r.217 is fixing, now stated as a lawn requirement too.
- **TWO FAILURE CONDITIONS, both stated as failures rather than preferences:** a ripple that moves the
  magazine one step, and **a friendly icon that takes a hit.** The second is DD-119's safety half in its
  mechanical form and belongs in the fire gate.
- **LAYER AND SECTOR ARE THE TWO MODES THAT ENFORCE EVERYTHING ELSE.** Sector enforces the arc written for
  that gun — which is why the siting record must carry an arc per gun, not just a position. Layer enforces
  class ownership — an Avenger refuses a mortar, an LPWS refuses a high track. Together they are the
  machinery behind DD-119's acceptance line.
- **THE SEAT MAP ADDS A FIFTH ROW TO WHAT DD-119 IMPLIED:** Avenger, LPWS, a ground seat **if one is
  recorded**, THAAD/Patriot for the high track, and Sentinel which never fires. *"Unless that seat was
  recorded for ground"* and *"if one is recorded"* both point at the same thing — **the siting record is
  what assigns class ownership**, not the code.
- **THE ORDER IS RESTATED AND IS NOW FOUR DEEP:** r.217 alone → the lawn record (every position, its arc,
  its class, its modes) → the lawn built from that record → **only then** these modes tested.

## Addendum (HI + Grok, 2026.10.19 — DD-122, the five-step proficiency ladder, and Real-World is locked)

Marked **HI + Grok**, so this is his as well as his tester's. Verbatim:

> The path is one sequence. **Proficiency is Real-World, and it stays locked until the four under it are on
> the record. A link, a menu, or a query string cannot open it.**
> The three original drills are the start of that record, not a separate game. **Each one is complete only
> when it has been run to the end and the metrics are written: hits, misses, rounds spent, and time. The
> magazine picture has to match the rounds. A run with no metrics does not count.**
>
> | Step | What it is | Opens the next only when |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Training** | The Range. Single shot. Train Up, then Train Down, then Qual 40 | All three are finished and the four metrics are stored |
> | **Introductory** | The lawn. Same three drills, one air track up, single shot | The same three are finished on the lawn, with metrics |
> | **Intermediate** | The three, plus **Pair**. Single and burst of 3. Air, then ground, each as its own run | Both classes are finished. **A wrong-class shot is a fail** |
> | **Advance** | **Mix, Leak, and Loss.** Shoot-look-shoot, ripple, sector, and layer | All three harder drills are finished. **A friendly icon hit is a fail. A threat left with no gun that can lock it is a fail** |
> | **Real-World** | Several up at once, across air, ground, and incoming rounds. Sentinel cues. Each seat takes only its own class | Advance is complete. **This is the only step that counts as proficiency** |
>
> Train Up, Train Down, and Qual 40 **do not appear again as a way around the ladder.** They are Training
> and Introductory. The harder drills are Intermediate and Advance. **They are not available from the first
> menu.**
> r.217 is still the toast and the share line. **This ladder is written into the record before a station
> moves, and the lock is tested by trying to open Real-World with an empty record. It must not open.**

### Reading — DD-122

- **THIS SUPERSEDES D-50's SHAPE WITHOUT CONTRADICTING ITS WORDS.** D-50 said *"the three, plus the harder
  ones"*. They are all still there — but **not alongside each other on one menu**. They are **rungs**: the
  three are Training (Range) and Introductory (lawn); the harder ones are Intermediate and Advance. *"They
  are not available from the first menu."*
- **REAL-WORLD IS A LOCK, AND THE LOCK IS THE TESTABLE PART.** *"A link, a menu, or a query string cannot
  open it."* That is three attack surfaces named explicitly, and the deck has all three — a `?mode=` reader,
  a select, and a deep link. **The acceptance test is written for me: try to open Real-World with an empty
  record; it must not open.** That is a red-first gate by construction.
- **A RUN ONLY COUNTS IF IT IS MEASURED.** Four metrics — **hits, misses, rounds spent, time** — and *"a
  run with no metrics does not count"*. So the ladder is gated on **recorded evidence**, not on having
  visited a screen. This is R-CORE's own doctrine applied to the player: the record is what makes a thing
  real.
- **"THE MAGAZINE PICTURE HAS TO MATCH THE ROUNDS" IS r.216's GATE, NOW LOAD-BEARING FOR PROGRESSION.**
  What began as a visual restoration is now the integrity check behind advancement — if the picture and
  the count disagree, the run's metrics cannot be trusted. That is the third time r.216 has turned out to
  underpin something downstream.
- **FOUR NEW DRILL NAMES ARRIVE AND NEED DEFINITION BEFORE THEY CAN BE BUILT:** **Pair** (Intermediate),
  and **Mix, Leak, Loss** (Advance). Mix and Loss can be inferred from DD-121 and the resilience tenet;
  **Leak** is genuinely unspecified. Recorded as owed rather than assumed.
- **THE FAILURE CONDITIONS NOW NUMBER FOUR, each attached to its rung:** a wrong-class shot (Intermediate),
  a friendly icon hit (Advance), **a threat left with no gun that can lock it** (Advance) — which is
  DD-119's acceptance line turned into a *scored* failure — and a ripple that moves the magazine once
  (DD-121).
- **THE ORDER IS NOW FIVE DEEP:** r.217 alone → this ladder into the record → the siting record (position,
  arc, class, modes) → the lawn built from both → the ladder and the modes tested, lock first.

## Addendum (operator + Grok, 2026.10.19 — DD-123, the white paper, and the ladder rewritten from it)

**His own white paper, carried into the record** as `docs/asks/2026.10.19_The_Future_of_Drone_Swarms_AI-4-Defense.pdf`
(1,625,266 B, 15 pages). **AI-4-Defense — Air Defense Leadership and Troops AI & Technology Briefing**,
prepared by **SSG SEGUIN, FIRES TEAM**, eXeL·HI. Read here, pages 1–8, not summarised from Grok.

**What the paper actually says, verified:**
- **Three phases, confirmed exactly as Grok read them:** *Enhanced Detection and Early Warning* · *Active
  Engagement and Neutralization* · *Survivability and Post-Engagement Recovery*. Three TTPs under each.
- **The threat taxonomy is in his own figure on page 0**, and it is the lawn's target list: **Large
  Aircraft · Medium UAS · Group 2 UAS · Small UAS · UGV · IVAS/Human** — feeding **JADC2 Edge AI**, through
  **Approvals** (a human icon) and **Risk + Effect**. **The human-in-the-loop approval is drawn in his own
  diagram**, which is where TARGET → APPROVE → FIRE comes from.
- **X-BAT is Shield AI's VTOL**, and the paper's own scenario is *"swarms of three groups of three X-BAT
  drones (nine total) in littoral and urban settings"* — **which is exactly the 9v9 rung of his roadmap.**
- Named TTPs that map onto the ladder: multi-spectrum sensor fusion (AN/TPY-2 + IR/EO), forward UAV
  pickets, AI-predictive tracking · tiered kinetic interceptors (**Patriot PAC-3 salvo**, ripple-fires), EW
  disruption, counter-swarms · dispersed positioning, redundant networks, rapid damage assessment.

**Grok's updated ladder, relayed verbatim:**

> The path is one sequence. Proficiency is Real-World, and it stays locked until the four under it are on
> the record. A link, a menu, or a query string cannot open it. **The full X-BAT swarm does not load until
> then.** That is the package at the end of Shield in the Sky: the next week starts only when the last one
> is complete.
> The three original drills are the start of the record… hits, misses, rounds spent, and time. The magazine
> picture has to match the rounds. A run with no metrics does not count.
> The briefings add the other half of the record. **The Future of Drone Swarms is three phases: Enhanced
> Detection, Active Engagement, Survivability. Shield in the Sky scores a fight in five lines: the missile
> threat is down, the low attackers are down, the swarm is down, the network is up, and the asset is still
> there. A person is never a target. NAS and human tracks are named so they can be held.**
>
> | Step | What it is | Opens the next only when |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Training** | The Range. Single shot. Train Up, then Train Down, then Qual 40 | All three finished, four metrics stored |
> | **Introductory** | **Detection.** The lawn. Same three drills, one air track up, single shot. **Sentinel must name it before Fire counts** | The three finished on the lawn with metrics, **and no shot was taken before the cue** |
> | **Intermediate** | The three, plus **Pair**. Single and a burst of 3. One class at a time: a small unmanned track, then a ground threat, each its own run | Both runs finished. A wrong-class shot is a fail. **A held track that was fired on is a fail** |
> | **Advance** | **Active Engagement.** Mix, Leak, Loss. Several up at once but **not yet a swarm**: one air track, one ground threat, one incoming round. Shoot-look-shoot, ripple, sector, layer. **Patriot may salvo only on the missile seat** | All three finished. Friendly icon hit = fail. A threat with no gun that can lock it = fail. **The five lines are stored** |
> | **Real-World** | **Survivability, then the swarm.** Several groups across air, ground and incoming rounds. **One gun is lost and the next gun that covers its dead ground takes the track.** Sentinel cues. Each seat its own class. **The asset is still there when it ends** | Advance complete. The only step that counts as proficiency. **The five lines must all read complete** |
>
> What each seat may take does not change… **A large aircraft, a medium unmanned track, a Group 2 or Group
> 3 track, a small unmanned track, and the X-BAT swarm are air. They are not five new seats. A boat is not
> this map.**
> …The lock is tested by trying to open Real-World, **and by trying to load the X-BAT swarm**, with an empty
> record. **Neither may open.**

### Reading — DD-123

- **THE LADDER IS NOW ANCHORED IN HIS OWN DOCTRINE, NOT INVENTED FOR THE GAME.** Introductory = Detection,
  Advance = Active Engagement, Real-World = Survivability. The three steps of the paper are the three upper
  rungs, in order.
- **THE CUE GATE IS NEW AND IS THE DETECTION PHASE MADE MECHANICAL:** *"Sentinel must name it before Fire
  counts"*, and **a shot taken before the cue fails the rung.** That is the paper's "layered sensor networks
  … detect before engage" expressed as a rule a player can break.
- **TWO MORE FAILURES ARRIVE:** *a held track that was fired on* (Intermediate) and the **five lines** as a
  stored result (Advance, Real-World). Running total: wrong-class shot · held track fired on · friendly
  icon hit · a threat no gun can lock · a ripple that moves the magazine once · and at the top, the asset
  not surviving.
- **RESILIENCE BECOMES PLAYABLE AT REAL-WORLD:** *"One gun is lost and the next gun that covers its dead
  ground takes the track."* That is FM 3-01's mutual-support tenet and the paper's dispersed-positioning
  TTP, turned into the thing the top rung tests. **It also means the siting record's dead-ground pairings
  are load-bearing for gameplay, not just for doctrine.**
- **THE AIR CLASS IS ONE CLASS WITH FIVE MEMBERS, NOT FIVE SEATS** — large aircraft, medium UAS, Group 2/3,
  small UAS, and the X-BAT swarm all go to an Avenger. *"A boat is not this map."* This prevents the seat
  count from inflating with the taxonomy.
- **"A PERSON IS NEVER A TARGET"** is now doctrine *and* code: **IVAS/Human is in his own figure**, so the
  human track exists in the data and must be **named so it can be HELD** — the Hold mode of DD-121 is what
  holds it. Together with "no friendly icon ever takes a hit", that is two protected classes.
- **THE LOCK NOW HAS TWO DOORS, and both are testable:** Real-World must not open with an empty record,
  **and the X-BAT swarm must not load.** Two red-first assertions.
- **NOT PROVIDED, AND RECORDED AS SUCH: "Shield in the Sky."** Grok cites it for the five lines and the
  week-by-week package; it is **not attached and not in the repo** (`grep` finds no copy). **The five lines
  are therefore taken from Grok's relay, not from a source I have read**, and that is marked rather than
  presented as verified. The Future of Drone Swarms I have read first-hand.
- **r.217 IS BOUNDED AGAIN:** toast and share line only.

### CORRECTION to DD-123 — "Shield in the Sky" is no longer unsourced

DD-123's Reading closed with: *"NOT PROVIDED, AND RECORDED AS SUCH … the five lines are therefore taken from
Grok's relay, not from a source I have read."* **The paper arrived in this message and that marking is
superseded.** Checked line for line against Asar's Synthesis in the paper itself:

> "All threads complete. **Primary missile threat destroyed. Low-altitude attackers eliminated. Drone swarm
> neutralized. Joint network stable. Defended assets preserved.**"

against the relay's *"the missile threat is down, the low attackers are down, the swarm is down, the network
is up, and the asset is still there."* **Five for five. The relay was accurate.** An append, not an edit:
DD-123 stands as written, with this correction after it.

### Reading — DD-124 (the ladder), what the paper ADDS that the ladder did not already carry

- **THE END PACKAGE IS LITERAL AND IS IN THE PAPER'S LAST PAGE.** *"The main display flashed and loaded the
  next package in cold light: FULL X-BAT SWARM SCENARIO LOADING … Week Two complete. Week Three starts
  now."* His *"the next week starts only when the last one is complete"* is a quotation of his own ending,
  so **the lock is the paper's own device**, not a rule added to it. Two red-first assertions unchanged:
  Real-World must not open on an empty record, and the swarm must not load.
- **THE LAYERED ASSIGNMENT IS NAMED THREE TIMES IN THE PAPER AND IS THE SITING LAW FOR THE LAWN:**
  Athena, twice — *"Patriot holds primary missile basket. M-SHORAD covers the inner ring. Avenger screens
  likely leakers in the low corridor"* and *"Patriot holds primary basket. Avenger left low corridor.
  M-SHORAD right flank. Preserve maneuver lanes. Cover leakers."* Aset states the principle bare: **"Outer
  ring for highest consequence. Inner ring for leakers. Guns for affordability."** That is a ring
  assignment, not a list of vehicles, and the lawn's stations inherit it.
- **THE DEFENDED ASSETS ARE NAMED, AND THEY ARE WHAT THE FIVE LINES SCORE.** Athena marks *"command post,
  fuel farm, ammunition transfer point, and maintenance cluster"*; Aset repeats *"Protect fuel. Protect
  ammunition. Protect command and control."* **So "the asset is still there" is not one object — it is a
  set, and the lawn needs its own set of defended points before Real-World can be scored at all.**
- **"PRIORITIZE BY THREAT TO MISSION, NOT BY NUMBER"** (Athena) is the ordering rule behind D-49's
  simultaneity. The player is not scored on how many he downs; he is scored on whether what mattered lived.
- **THE EXCHANGE-RATIO PROBLEM IS IN THE PAPER AS AN UNRESOLVED TENSION, NOT A SOLVED ONE.** Thor:
  *"Still bad math."* Asar: *"Not if the mission survives."* **A metric that counts rounds spent (his own
  four) is therefore meaningful at every rung**, and "guns for affordability" is the in-game reason a burst
  exists at all.
- **A SECOND PROTECTED CLASS IS CONFIRMED BY THE NARRATIVE, NOT ONLY BY THE TAXONOMY.** *"No fratricide risk
  within basket"* (Enlil) and *"Verify targets"* (Thought Master) sit beside his own "a person is never a
  target". Held, never shot.
- **DETECTION BEFORE ENGAGEMENT IS THE PAPER'S OPENING MOVE** — Enki builds the picture before anything
  fires; Sentinel sweeps before the Avenger slews. That is why Introductory's cue gate (*"Sentinel must
  name it before Fire counts"*) is the second rung and not a later refinement.
- **"DO NOT CHASE EVERY GHOST. STAY WITH DEFENDED ASSET LOGIC"** is the failure mode LEAK should test, and
  is the nearest thing in either paper to a definition of it. **LEAK is still not defined by him** and
  remains owed — but the paper narrows it: a leaker is a track that passes the outer layer and must be met
  by the inner ring, and chasing a decoy is how the leaker gets its lane.
- **THE TWELVE CALL SIGNS ARE HIS OWN ROSTER AND THEY MATCH THE AsM FLEET EXACTLY** — Enki, Thor, Krishna,
  Odin, Enlil, Athena, Sofia, Aset, Pangu, Christo, Thoth, Asar, under Battle Commander **Thought Master**.
  The closing roll call assigns each one a verb: *"Enki sparked. Thor shielded. Krishna connected. Odin
  foresaw. Enlil built. Athena directed. Sofia clarified. Aset restored. Pangu innovated. Christo unified.
  Thoth measured. Asar completed."* **That is the fleet's own charter in his words**, and the 48-agent fleet
  of DD-125 is organised on it.
- **THE DOCTRINE LIST AT THE BACK IS NEW SOURCE MATERIAL AND IS WIDER THAN FM 3-01:** FM 3-01 · FM 3-27 ·
  ATP 3-01.7 · ADA Reference Handbook · ADP 3-0 · **ATP 3-01.81 (Counter-UAS techniques and swarm defense)**
  · FM 3-12 (jamming, sensing, EW pressure) · JP 3-01 · JADC2 Strategy · Shield AI X-BAT docs · USMC
  concepts · DoD AI Strategy. **ATP 3-01.81 is the manual the lawn's counter-UAS drills should be read
  against**, and it had not been named before this paper.
- **ELECTRONIC WARFARE IS IN THE FIGHT AND IS NOT IN THE LADDER.** Pangu's *"spoof package active, swarm
  cohesion degrading"* is a non-kinetic effect that changes the swarm's behaviour. **Recorded as present in
  his source and ABSENT from the five rungs** — his call whether it enters, and at which rung.
- **ONE TENSION WORTH HIS EYE:** the paper's Patriot is on the field and firing the primary basket, while
  DD-124's seat map says *"THAAD and Patriot stay off the lawn and take the high track."* Both are his
  words from the same message. Read as: the paper is the desert battery; **the lawn is a different site and
  the high-tier seats are not on its grass** — they are reachable as a high track, not as a station a player
  stands on. One word changes it.

### Reading — DD-125 (the fleet), as an instruction about how work is done here

- **IT IS A STRUCTURE, NOT A MOOD.** 12 AsM × 3 specialists = 36, plus 12 MoT = **48**, in **3 pods of 4
  AsMs**. Within each triad, all three produce independent work and **the third is the Voting & Summarizer**
  — it forms its own view, then collects, votes and synthesises into the AsM's single package.
- **"REPORT ONLY FLEET READINESS CONFIRMATION AND ANY RESIDUAL GAPS"** bounds the report, not the work. The
  work's deliverable is the Capitol-lawn plan he asked for in the same breath.
- **HE CALLS IT "THE LOCKED, PROVEN INITIATOR"** and asks that it be pasted first in any new session after a
  reset. **Recorded here so it survives a container restart**, which has already killed this fleet twice.
- **IT ARRIVES WITH A SUBJECT**: assess the Capitol of Texas lawn against *today's inputs* (DD-111 → DD-124)
  and *both* white papers, and generate the plan. **Deep thinking is the explicit ask** — *"as deep level
  thinking is required for more realistic training and engagement."*

### Reading — DD-126

- **"PLACE THIS AMBITIOUS TRAINING FOR REAL-WORLD MODE" IS A DESIGN INSTRUCTION, NOT A FILING ONE.** The
  paper's fight — the one Asar scores in five lines — **is** the Real-World rung's scenario. DD-124 already
  made Real-World *"Survivability, then the swarm"* and gated it on those five lines; this names the fight
  itself. **The TOC narrative is the script**: Enki builds the picture, Thor classifies hostile-until-disproven,
  Krishna fuses, a low-slow probe comes first, a saturation pattern forms, a fast track reclassifies as a cruise
  missile, Patriot takes the basket while the guns hold the low corridor, a leaker gets over the wire, a spoof
  package degrades the swarm, Aset rebuilds the layer, one last stubborn low track, then the five lines.
- **IT ARRIVES WITH ITS OWN SEQUENCE, AND THAT SEQUENCE IS THE SCENARIO'S PHASES.** Enki's Spark (detection) →
  Thor's Shield (classification and the saturation build) → Krishna's Thread (fusion and the kill-chain order)
  → Odin's Eye (the primary engagement) → Enlil's Order (the low fight and the first leaker) → Athena's
  Direction (posture shift, the southern seam) → Sofia's Lens (the coordinated second attack) → Aset's Echo
  (restore the line, the last track) → Asar's Synthesis (the five lines, then the swarm package loads). **Nine
  beats, each with a named decision** — that is a scenario script, not a mood.
- **TWELVE NAMED SEATS, AND HE HAS SAID TWICE THEY ARE THE FLEET.** The closing roll call assigns a verb to
  each. Real-World on the lawn is therefore the first rung where the twelve are not a review fleet but
  **positions on the glass**.
- **THE PLACEMENT, MEASURED HERE, AND WHAT IS AND IS NOT TRUE OF IT:**
  - **The bucket is real and the pattern is the house's own.** `frontend/app/vision-2525/page.tsx:81` links
    `…/storage/v1/object/public/whitepaper/VISION-2525.pdf`, and
    `.github/workflows/upload-vision-pdf.yml` pushes it there **from `docs/whitepaper/VISION-2525.pdf` in git**,
    then checks the public URL answers. So Grok put the file in exactly the right place.
  - **But the half that makes it durable is missing.** There is **no `docs/whitepaper/Shield-in-the-Sky.pdf` in
    git**, the workflow's `paths:` trigger names VISION-2525.pdf only, and **nothing in the repo links the new
    object**. So it is an upload, not a publication: nothing regenerates it, nothing gates it, and if the bucket

  - **I could not read it from here.** `curl -sSI` on that URL returns `CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403` —
    the sandbox proxy refuses every host, which is the same reason Verify Live rather than this container is
    what proves the live site. **So "Claude can read it at this address" is not true of this container**, and no
    gate can depend on it. Recorded, not argued: Grok's intent is right and the mechanism does not reach me.
  - **What I CAN read and gate is already in the record:** he pasted the whole paper and it is carried at
    `docs/asks/2026.10.19_Shield_in_the_Sky_ADA_Briefing.md`, 26,867 B, sha256
    `ad8f928024343a3c1b1d8260bb71f967b683daf0b9bf483cf2f8ae8c4a3d5d94`, in the canonical manifest and verified

## Addendum 15 — DD-127 (operator, 2026-10-19, verbatim) — EW is electronic warfare, usually jamming

> EW is electronic warefare usually jamming

### Reading — DD-127

- **HE IS NAMING THE GENERAL CASE, AND IT POINTS THE OTHER WAY FROM WHERE I HAD IT.** I had summarised EW as
  *"the spoof package"* — Pangu's line in his paper, *"Spoofing package active. Swarm cohesion degrading."*
  **Spoofing is deception; jamming is denial.** They are not the same technique, and his sentence says the
  general case is **jamming**. So EW in this project is read first as **jamming — denial of the sensor and the
  link — and only second as the one spoof effect the paper happens to name.**
- **AND JAMMING IS ALREADY IN HIS PAPER AS A THREAT, NOT ONLY AS A TOOL OF OURS.** Thor's threat rings open
  with it: *"Cruise missiles, UAS, **jamming**, saturation avenues. Cover every approach or something burns."*
  Mid-fight it comes again: *"similar UAS activity, possible low flyers, **probing electronic attack**."* And
  Sofia says what it is for, twice: *"They want cognition first, casualties second"* and *"They're overloading
  operators, not just radars."* **FM 3-12 is in his own back matter for exactly this.** So the paper has EW on
  BOTH sides and I had only recorded ours.
- **THIS IS WHY IT BELONGS AT REAL-WORLD AND WHAT IT ACTUALLY DOES THERE.** Jamming against us is not a weapon
  the player operates — **it is a condition on the glass**: the Sentinel cue goes intermittent, tracks flicker
  or drop, the picture degrades. That lands directly on the rung's own machinery rather than adding any:
  Introductory's cue gate (*"Sentinel must name it before Fire counts"*) becomes a real decision when the cue
  is late or missing, and the paper's own answer is the lesson — *"Machines detect. Networks share. Humans
  decide what matters."*
- **IT ANSWERS ONE OF THE THREE QUESTIONS I PUT TO HIM.** I had recorded *"Joint network stable"* as NOT
  scoreable on a deck that fetches nothing (DD-126). **With jamming as the EW model it becomes scoreable
  without any network at all:** the line means **the shared picture held under jamming** — cue availability
  and track continuity across the run, measured from the deck's own rows. The fourth of Asar's five lines
  therefore has a definition now, and it is his, not mine.
- **STILL HIS CALL, NARROWED:** whether jamming enters only at Real-World (my reading, since Introductory
  through Advance are meant to be clean runs of their own drills) or earlier as a difficulty; and whether the
  player ever has an EW effect of his own — the paper's spoof package degrading swarm cohesion — or only ever
  receives it. **Recorded as two questions, not assumed either way.**
- **r.217 IS UNAFFECTED** — toast and share line only.

It closes the first pass by correcting earlier mismatches: the three range drills (TRAIN UP, TRAIN DOWN, QUAL 40) are live only on the Range and are not yet playable on the lawn; QUAL 40 belongs to Training, not Advance; and the Real-World swarm is decided at nine tracks in three groups of three rather than forty-two. The ladder is the only path that counts—Training on the Range, then Introductory on the lawn, Intermediate, Advance, and Real-World proficiency—and a higher step cannot open until the line above it is complete. UCRS-2525 bearings replace degrees, with 0000 and 3600 as north and arcs written as four-digit pairs.
> The seat table locks nine lines: four air seats that appear progressively (A1 in Introductory, A2 and A3 in Intermediate, A4 in Advance), two cue-only Sentinels, two incoming-round seats, one ground seat, and one high-track seat that appears only in Real-World. Fire modes split between Range and lawn selectors; the three presses remain in front of every mode, and the lawn does not use the Range mode control. Real-World consists of nine designed beats (Enki’s Spark through Asar’s Synthesis). The gate is exactly five scored lines—primary missile threat destroyed, low-altitude attackers eliminated, drone swarm neutralized, joint network stable, and defended assets preserved—after which the X-BAT package may load. “All threads complete” names the synthesis and is not a sixth line.
> Three blockers remain unwaived: capitol façades are still shootable pop-ups, the four defended assets exist only on paper, and LPWS still has no icon. Modular interfaces (hardware-in-the-loop, sensor-in-the-loop, edge, robotics) and R-CORE modes are scaffolded or planned but must not alter the three presses, the seat table, or the nine beats. Where the plan or architecture files differ from this CRS, this file wins. The lawn drills are not live, the swarm count is decided, and Real-World stays locked until the ladder and blockers are resolved.
> Drone-2525_Plan_Introductory_Mode_Build.md (updated with scaling targets)
> This plan translates the CRS into an ordered build sequence for Claude Code. Training remains the foundation on the Range. Introduction is the first lawn work and must complete the path from one player to two on one system, then 1v1, then 3v3 before any later level advances. Intermediate adds saturation, bait, and the high-track rule drawn from Shield beats 2–4. Advanced keeps QUAL 40 live and adds leaker and seam coverage from beats 5–6. Real-World remains designed: nine beats gated on Asar’s five lines, with façades made non-shootable and the four defended assets present before the X-BAT package can load. Input and Output columns are still placeholders for Claude Code to fill so gaps against the CRS stay visible.
> The near-term gate is explicit: 3v3 equals six air systems supporting up to twelve HI pilots (two per system). This is the Introductory completion target. The long-term scaling target is 21v21, or forty-two HI pilots versus forty-two HI pilots, corresponding to the old even-ring number that the CRS retired as a gun or first-build swarm count. The current first-build Real-World swarm stays at nine tracks in three groups of three. The plan therefore distinguishes the six-air / twelve-HI Introductory gate, the nine-seat first-build Real-World, and the eventual forty-two-versus-forty-two capacity.
> Build order is strict: Training solid, then Introduction through 3v3, then Intermediate only after Introduction is green, Advanced only after Intermediate, and Real-World only after Advanced plus the façade and asset blockers are resolved. The gap check confirms alignment with the CRS on Training, Introduction, QUAL 40, and the Real-World gate, while Intermediate and full Advanced seam coverage remain partial. Doctrine anchors are FM 3-01, ATP 3-01.81, and FM 3-12. The file asks Claude Code to fill every Input/Output pair in the same column order as the CRS so mismatches are caught by direct comparison rather than by later discovery.

nose +x to the heading. (3) r.223 → r.224 → the play-through continue as planned; the small (demo 1.111 m) foil on the
lawn is the accepted start for Capital Texas lawn training. The boot row gains a rotation leg (nose right of tail at

## Addendum 39 — DD-150 (operator, 2026-10-22, verbatim) — LINK-2525 protocols with the light codex per R-CORE for inter-aircraft comms; the Link 16 → Link-2525 architecture; the whole programme is an R-CORE demo of the five Vision-2525 protocol layers

> Ensure LINK-2525 protocols with light codex are used per R-CORE for inter aircraft comms
> remember link 16 currently operates on 512x3 (A-B) architecture while Link-2525 (that evolves) will initially operate on 3600 x 7 (A-G)
> everything we do is to build R-CORE demo of Link-2525, Sync-2525, Comm-2525, Edge-2525, and UCRS-2525

**Reading (DD-150).** The inter-aircraft / inter-system communication — the wire between the two phones (systems) in the
lawn room: the WebRTC data channel, the lawn verbs (CSTART/CTIME/CRESET/CUE) and the team-code pairing carried by the deck's
**light codex** (`lc4Encode`/`lc4Decode`, `LC_LEVELS`, `state.codex`) — is to be framed and built per **LINK-2525 protocols,
using the light codex, under R-CORE**. The architecture evolves from the legacy reference: **Link 16 = 512 × 3 (A–B)** — a
two-value (A–B) channel over 512 — while **Link-2525 (evolving) initially = 3600 × 7 (A–G)** — the **3600** of the UCRS-2525
bearing circle (0000/3600 north, 1800 south) × **seven levels A–G** (echoing the deck's seven-rung LIGHT CODEX / `LC_LEVELS`).
And the overarching frame: **everything in this programme is to build an R-CORE demo of the five Vision-2525 protocol layers** —
**COMM-2525** (the signal fabric), **LINK-2525** (coordination continuity — the mission carries its context), **EDGE-2525**
(the point of need — the device holds the work), **SYNC-2525** (operational truth — the append-only record / the replay hash),
and **UCRS-2525** (one spatial language — the UCRS bearings). How this touches the work in flight: r.228/r.229's inter-system
wire already demonstrates LINK-2525 (the record travels between systems) and SYNC-2525 (one replay hash on both phones =
operational truth preserved), over the light codex's paired channel. It connects to DD-41 (the comms read as crypto / COMM-2525,
not a "light code") — the pending comms-reframing revision — and to the R-CORE record that already names these layers
(`RCORE_SYS.COMM`). The 3600×7 (A–G) encoding is the forward spec for LINK-2525's own addressing; it is persisted here and kept
for the comms layer, not retrofitted into r.229 (H03), whose wire is already LINK-2525/SYNC-2525 in shape. No row is reopened;
this sets the naming and the architecture the comms work is built to.

## Addendum 41 — DD-152 (operator, 2026-10-23, verbatim) — update the R-CORE 5 pillars per all learnings; Drone only the current revision; the training modes restated; come to a stopping spot for an urgent Architect-2525 evolution

> update R-CORE 5 pillars per all learnings of all 2525 projects
> regarding drone-2525, only update that revision since that's what we are working currently
> remember Train up is default on The Range, and other training locations. All targets are up without start and firing knocks down and it pops up. Train down has start button and timer to knock all targets down and Qual-40 is military grade annual qualification
> Hows Drone-2525; come to a good stopping spot. We have an urgent evolution to do on Architect-2525

**Reading (DD-152).** The "R-CORE 5 pillars" are the five systems — **COMM-2525 · LINK-2525 · EDGE-2525 · SYNC-2525 · UCRS-2525** (Vision • 2525 §III–VII), defined in `frontend/lib/2525-core/controls.ts`. They carried a bare name→code map and no learnings. This update adds, as `RCORE_PILLARS` beside the untouched `RCORE_SYS`, each pillar's role and one grounded learning synthesised from every 2525 project (COMM = independent providers not code paths, the Trinity-of-one-vendor lesson; LINK = no cross-talk, the r.229 lawn room, Link 16 512×3 → Link-2525 3600×7; EDGE = the one-file offline deck and the signer's phone; SYNC = append-only record + replay hash + the training modes; UCRS = the AAAA.BBBB bearings), plus the fidelity law (a shot is scored from the shared state, never the picture). Gated by `controls-schema` and documented in `docs/MODE_R-CORE_SPEC.md`. **Drone-2525 is scoped to the current revision only** — the pillars update is shared source; the Drone deck stays at r.229 (no new deck revision, no rewrite of its history). The training modes are captured verbatim into SYNC's learning: Train Up is the default on the Range and every training location — all targets up, no start, a hit knocks a target down and it pops back up (infinite practice); Train Down has a start button and a timer to knock all targets down; Qual-40 is the military-grade annual qualification. Drone-2525 is at a clean stopping spot (r.229 LIVE, the SensorFusion launcher tile LIVE, nothing in flight); the next work is the urgent Architect-2525 evolution, whose specifics are awaited.
